THREAD A, the S4 re-execution: complete the screening never completed on the 700 X11 records (eligibility UNDECIDED), then read full texts for the strand's core records, rewrite section 8.4 on that evidence, and re-ground the branch agenda. THREAD B: close repository reproducibility gaps VG-14 and VG-16. THREAD C: verify load-bearing explosive-regime locators, boundary stated, NOT a full 60-record sweep. THREAD D: refute-gate calibration memo. FOCUS FOR THIS ROUND: (a) THE CENTRAL QUESTION — the completion screen fixed an S4 subset rule in advance (sha256 5f64d29b6868670c, embedded in the verdict file), assessed 197 X11 records BY READING with the keyword classifier never invoked, and returned 178 include / 12 X5 / 7 undecided, taking the corpus from 149 to 327. Verify the rule was genuinely fixed before assessment (mtimes, embedded digest, git HEAD), that the verdicts are read-based rather than classifier-derived, that the subset rule follows from the frozen protocol passages it cites (section 1.3 S4 row, section 2.2 C3 clause and transfer clause) rather than being reverse-engineered to a convenient subset size, and whether 178 includes is defensible or whether the screen was too permissive. The screen deliberately never applied X6, on the reasoning that include is a positive existence claim an abstract can support while X6 asserts a record states NEITHER payoff support NOR an applicability condition — adjudicate that reasoning. (b) The screening agent introduced TWO false claims into its own amendment prose and caught both inside the stage: an 8,663 figure that should have been 7,418, and a coverage claim that the DOI landing-page arm had run for all 88 records when it had run for 30 — it then completed the arm for the remaining 58 rather than softening the sentence. Verify both corrections landed and check the amendment prose for any third. (c) Extraction depth honesty: 59 core records attempted at full text, 33 obtained and read, 26 not obtained (21 abs, 5 meta), and 178 newly-included records carried at a screen-abstract label explicitly defined as an eligibility-screen depth and NOT an extraction depth with extraction_performed false. Verify no section 8.4 claim asserts an applicability condition at abstract depth without saying so, and that the 26 inline depth markers are complete and correct. (d) THE SUBSTANTIVE RESEARCH CORRECTION: the full texts establish that Glosten-Milgrom and Kyle have NO inventory objective (GM: zero expected profit per trade, with a footnote that binding inventory constraints would break the result; Kyle: price = E[v given order flow], no inventory state), so agenda Branch 2's H0 was VACUOUS for both, and the model set is corrected to Ho-Stoll / Avellaneda-Stoikov / Gueant et al. Verify this against the cited passages — it is the session's most consequential claim and it reverses a prior reading. (e) A frozen arithmetic identity NOW FAILS and is reported as failing: len(bibliography store) == n_included is 149 vs 327, recorded as gap AG-11. Adjudicate whether reporting rather than repairing is right, and whether every downstream count that used 149 is now correctly restated. (f) The flow: X1 6707 + X2 338 + X3 35 + X5 106 + X7 244 + X9 1 + X10 545 + X11 510 + include 327 = 8,813, with read-based 347 vs classifier 8,466 (96.1%). The lead verified this arithmetic; verify the ALLOCATION, particularly the 138/59 stratum split of the 197 which the agent states is derived from the arms field and explicitly flags as not independently re-run. (g) Thread B: tests/test_erob_recount.py must RE-DERIVE the ER-RoB domain column from the extraction JSONLs, not restate the shipped table. The lead independently mutated an input (eru-0131 Q6 yes to no) and confirmed three assertions fail and the lenient count moves 31 to 32. Verify the test cannot pass on a hardcoded table, that its expected-divergence set matches amendment A8 rather than being widened to fit, and check the recorded finding that A8's PROSE misdescribes what its 31 is counted against (resolved column, not the primary extractor's raw judgments, which give 42). Also verify ADR-0005's claim that tracking uv.lock does NOT fully close VG-14. (h) Thread C: the load-bearing set is 50 items over 21 records, 35 verified / 7 corrected / 8 unverifiable, and one correction REVERSED a round-3 correction that was itself wrong (PWY Appendix pp.222-225 to pp.222-224). Another found a review claim contradicted by its source (a crash monitor said to never signal before the crash by construction, where the authors state the FPR has no closed form and further theoretical analysis is not possible). Verify a sample of the seven corrections and that the boundary is honestly drawn rather than a partial sweep reported as complete. (i) Thread D: the memo concludes H2-strong (refuters going through the motions) is DISCONFIRMED — 140 retained findings, zero empty refutation fields, four refutations correcting the auditor against its own interest — while H1 is supported but not established and the two do not separate for want of ground truth. It reports nine schema/durability defects, of which D6 is that 13 of 17 pinned path-digest pairs do not exist in git history. Verify the evidence_type distribution and D6 against the trails. (j) FOUR RETRIEVALS ACROSS TWO THREADS RETURNED HTTP 200 WITH application/pdf FOR THE WRONG PAPER. Verify that every full-text claim in this session rests on an identity-checked file.

# Audit trail — S4 re-execution and repository reproducibility gaps, round 1

Round 1 of a fresh three-round cap over the four-thread deliverable [deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md](../deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md): Thread A (the S4 completion screen and full-text re-execution), Thread B (repository gaps VG-14 and VG-16), Thread C (load-bearing explosive-regime locator verification, boundary declared) and Thread D (the refute-gate calibration memo). Seven specialist branches ran in parallel; every critical and major finding then passed an adversarial refute gate before remediation was authorised. All seven branches returned `proceed-with-remediation`. The machine-readable payload for this round is the JSON sidecar named in the front matter and is held there verbatim (FAIR I1).

## findings-table

One row per finding, carrying all eight schema fields (id, severity, category, location, issue, evidence, fix, reference) plus the raising branch and the disposition. Long cells are compressed here with `…`; the unabridged text of every field is in the JSON sidecar, and every gated claim is reproduced verbatim and unabridged in [refute-gate](#refute-gate) below. Totals: **1 critical, 21 major, 36 minor** raised; **2 refuted at the gate**; **20 routed to remediation**; **36 minors logged, not remediated**; **0 retained-conservative**.

## refute-gate

Every critical and major finding passed the adversarial refute gate before remediation; a drop requires concrete counter-evidence of an enumerated type, never bare disagreement ([arXiv 2604.19049](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19049)). Twenty-two dispositions: **2 dropped** (REV-1-1, SCOPE-1-1) and **20 retained** after failed refutation attempts. `retained_conservative: 0` — no finding was retained merely for want of an attempted refutation; every retention below carries the refuter's own account of what it tried and why it failed. Both drops carry concrete counter-evidence (`source-quote` for REV-1-1, `reproduced-check` for SCOPE-1-1), so neither is a bare-disagreement drop. Three entries carry `reproduction_required: true` — SCOPE-1-1 (dropped), REV-1-5 (retained) and CODE-1-1 (retained); their `reproduction: {command, observed}` blocks are lifted from the refutation evidence and given with those entries. Claims are reproduced verbatim and unabridged, and retained-after-failed-refutation entries are included alongside the drops.

### REV-1-1 — dropped (refuted)

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: critical; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **source-quote**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **dropped**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** design
> **location:** docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2156-2171 (A16 §(d)); docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl (all 178 include rows)
> **issue:** The 178 include verdicts cite the frozen transfer clause as their I1 warrant, but the stage never applied the clause's test. Frozen I1 admits a C3 model record only 'under the section 2.2 transfer clause', and that clause is CONJUNCTIVE: eligible only if the corpus can state what the record's own text says about its payoff support AND applicability conditions. A16 §(d) notices the divergence between that bar and X6's disjunctive bar, and resolves it by applying only X6 — i.e. by admitting a record whose abstract states EITHER an assumed environment OR a condition under which its result holds. The justification offered ('X-codes are the protocol's operative exclusion mechanism') does not engage with the fact that I1 is an INCLUSION criterion whose text carries the clause; failing an inclusion criterion is grounds for a non-include verdict independent of any X-code. Three mechanical consequences confirm the bar dropped rather than being applied: (a) 'payoff support' appears in exactly 5 of the 198 lines of the verdict file, and all five are in `undecided` rows — ZERO of the 178 include rows records the payoff support the clause requires the corpus to be able to state, so even the 'can state' precondition is unevidenced per record; (b) the `criterion_cited` field is an identical boilerplate string on every include row and asserts satisfaction of a clause no row substantiates; (c) ZERO X6 verdicts were returned, so inside the subset the only reachable exclusion code was X5 — the stage was structurally incapable of excluding any record on the transfer clause. This is the direct cause of the 178/197 = 90.4% include rate and of the corpus moving 149 -> 327, and it defeats A16 §(a)'s own stated reason for splitting SR-1 from the eligibility test ('no record could ever receive the one verdict the frozen protocol wrote for this literature'). A16 nevertheless asserts 'No eligibility criterion is altered.'
> **evidence:** A16 §(d): "The transfer clause admits a C3 record \"only if the corpus can state what the record's own text says about its payoff support **and** applicability conditions\", while X6 excludes a C3 record that \"states **neither** payoff support **nor** an applicability condition\". Those are different bars. **X6 as written in section 2.4 was applied**, because X-codes are the protocol's operative exclusion mechanism." — and the include rows' uniform warrant: "criterion_cited": "I1 via the section 2.2 transfer clause; I2 via C3; I3 satisfied by construction of the X11 stratum (the classifier routed records without a persistent identifier to X7); I4 satisfied at abstract depth"
> **fix:** Do one of three things and say which. (1) Preferred: record, per include row, the payoff support AND the applicability condition the record's own text states — that is what the transfer clause asks the corpus to be able to do, and it is the only warrant that makes the cited criterion true. Rows that cannot carry both move to `undecided inside the subset`, as U00228 already does. (2) Or re-code the affected includes to `undecided` pending that evidence and restate n_included, n_excluded and the 149 -> 327 headline against the reduced set. (3) Or, if the disjunctive reading is kept, append a numbered amendment that states plainly that the transfer clause's conjunction was replaced by a disjunction for this stage, retract 'No eligibility criterion is altered', report how many of the 178 rest on only one conjunct, and carry that qualification into the review's §6 and §7 preambles and the frontmatter's `n_included` note — none of which currently mention it.
> **reference:** CHAMP items 1, 3 and 6 (goal/design validity; consistency with the frozen protocol); frozen protocol §2.2 transfer clause and criterion I1 as printed in the review frontmatter lines 9-10; deliverable spec Thread A item 1 check ('every row cites a criterion')

Refutation (counter-evidence, `source-quote`):

> The finding's mechanical counts reproduce, but its legal premise is contradicted by frozen protocol text it never cites: section 2.5 rule J4.
>
> VERBATIM, docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md lines 310-313:
> "**J4 - market-making records on unbounded instruments.** Decided entirely by the transfer clause in 2.2 plus X6. No separate judgement is exercised: state applicability conditions and the record is in, with transfer status recorded; state none and X6 applies."
>
> Provenance verified myself: J4 begins at byte offset 19,899 of the file; I recomputed sha256 over the first 82,677 bytes and got 99524df02696a59ab878173ce7f3636d9cd07067b5a801a677cdf5d126fa13f4, matching the registered frozen-prefix digest (commit 27d7473, and the `_header` of both S4 log files). J4 is therefore pre-registered frozen text, inside the frozen prefix, and no amendment touches it (grep "J4" returns exactly one hit in the protocol).
>
> J4 defeats the finding's three load-bearing propositions:
> 1. "the stage never applied the clause's test" / "the bar dropped rather than being applied." J4 governs precisely this population - C3 model records on unbounded (non-2.1) instruments - and fixes its decision procedure as "the transfer clause in 2.2 plus X6." The clause's operative test, per the frozen protocol's own composition, IS the X6 test. Applying X6 is applying the clause, not substituting for it. The clause's own final sentence agrees: "A C3 record that states no applicability condition and no payoff-support assumption is excluded under X6" (line 207-208) - i.e. the clause itself states its exclusion in the "neither" form, so the "divergence" A16 flags is internal to the clause and J4 resolves it.
> 2. "failing an inclusion criterion is grounds for a non-include verdict independent of any X-code." Contradicted verbatim: "No separate judgement is exercised." J4 forecloses exactly the independent I1 judgement the finding demands.
> 3. "A16 nevertheless asserts 'No eligibility criterion is altered'" (offered as a false assertion). A16 lists "I1-I6, X1-X9, B-a-B-d, C1-C4 and J1-J6 are applied exactly as frozen in section 2." J4 is in that set, and A16 sec (d)'s reading is what J4 prescribes: state applicability conditions -> in; state neither -> X6. Under J4 the assertion is true.
>
> The finding's cited reason for the SR-1 split is likewise preserved rather than defeated: J4's "state none and X6 applies" is literally "the one verdict the frozen protocol wrote for this literature," and A5 (lines 1449) had already reserved X6 as the code that "asserts the record states no applicability condition."
>
> Counts I reproduced (they are accurate but do not establish the defect): ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl = 198 lines, 1 header + 197 verdicts, Counter{include 178, exclude 12, undecided 7}; primary_code Counter{None 178, X5 12, X11 7} - zero X6, as claimed; "payoff support" occurs on exactly 5 lines, all undecided rows (U00228, U01782, U03638, U03788, U07469). One sub-claim is wrong: criterion_cited is NOT identical on all 178 - it is identical on 177, and U01611 cites "I1 directly," so only 177 includes rest on the transfer clause at all.
>
> I also checked whether the corpus states applicability conditions per include row, since J4's inclusion branch requires that. A 12-row random sample of include rationales all state an assumed environment or condition (e.g. U01376 "states the conditions (absence of adverse selection, profit volatility) under which its results hold"; U07813 "risk neutrality and perfect competition ... existence and uniqueness under stated conditions"; U03867 stochastic FX holdings; U04123 stochastic volatility, jumps, Hawkes dynamics). So the J4 inclusion branch is substantiated on its face.
>
> One residual I record honestly, because it is real but is NOT the alleged defect: J4's clause "with transfer status recorded" is unmet - ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl carries E10 = {"transfer_status": "not extracted", "transfer_status_reason": "Eligibility was decided at abstract depth by amendment A16. Extraction was not performed..."} for all 178, extraction_performed=false, depth_reached "screen-abstract". That is an extraction-completeness gap already disclosed in the open by A17, and it does not make the 178 include verdicts unwarranted, does not require re-coding them to undecided, and does not invalidate the 149 -> 327 headline - which are what REV-1-1 claims and what its three proposed remedies would enact.

### SCOPE-1-1 — dropped (refuted)

- raised_by: scope-auditor; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **reproduced-check**; reproduction_required: **true**; outcome: **dropped**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** partial
> **location:** docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md:34; repository root uv.lock
> **issue:** Thread B item 1 has two halves — `uv.lock` tracked, and the pinning decision recorded. The decision half shipped (ADR-0005). The tracking half is not evidenced: the repository state visible to this audit lists `uv.lock` as an untracked working-tree file, while every other artifact this session produced is absent from the untracked list. ADR-0005 nevertheless asserts the completed state in the present tense.
> **evidence:** Spec check, Thread B item 1: "`git ls-files uv.lock` returns the path; the decision appears in a tracked file with its rationale." ADR-0005 §Decision: "**`uv.lock` is the pinning mechanism and is tracked.**" and "`uv.lock` is committed. It resolves the full dependency graph — 1,467 lines". Repository status at audit time: `?? uv.lock` (untracked), whereas `docs/decisions/ADR-0005-*.md`, `tests/test_erob_recount.py`, `ka-s4-*.jsonl` and the memo do not appear as untracked, indicating the session's other artifacts reached the index and this one did not. ADR-0005 §Context itself describes the pre-state as "`uv.lock` was present in the working tree but **untracked**" — the same state the audit observes.
> **fix:** Either `git add uv.lock` and include it in the Thread E commit before the deliverable is ticked, or, if it is deliberately deferred, restate ADR-0005 §Decision in the future tense and record the tick as not met. Do not tick Thread B item 1 on the ADR alone: the ADR is the decision, not the pin.
> **reference:** S-6 (Thread B, `uv.lock` tracked, and the pinning decision recorded)

Refutation (counter-evidence, `reproduced-check`):

> Ran the spec check itself. `git ls-files -s uv.lock` returns `100644 3f3e2b1b5cc12bf4213b20b3069a75ed55e6fd7e 0	uv.lock` — the path resolves, satisfying Thread B item 1's stated check verbatim. It is committed, not merely staged: `git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock` succeeds, and `git log --oneline --diff-filter=A -- uv.lock` identifies the adding commit as `2ba291f build: track uv.lock as the pinning mechanism; ADR-0005; declare s4-reexecution session`, one commit behind current HEAD `01ecfe7`. `git diff HEAD --stat -- uv.lock` is empty, so the committed blob equals the working tree. `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` does NOT list uv.lock. ADR-0005 line 34's present-tense assertion is accurate.
>
> Source of the auditor's error: it relied on the session-start `gitStatus` snapshot showing `?? uv.lock` at HEAD `dd4d151`, a state predating commit `2ba291f`; that snapshot is explicitly documented as not updating during the session.
>
> The finding's supporting premise is additionally inverted. It claimed the session's other artifacts "do not appear as untracked... indicating [they] reached the index and this one did not." The live untracked list is the reverse: `docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl`, `ka-s4-subset-rule.md`, `docs/literature/search_logs/explosive-regime/se-verify-loadbearing-01.json`, and `docs/research_notes/memo_refute-gate-calibration_2026-09-03.md` are all still `??`, while `uv.lock`, `docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md`, and `tests/test_erob_recount.py` are tracked. uv.lock is on the tracked side of that partition.
>
> Both halves of Thread B item 1 shipped: the pin (uv.lock in HEAD) and the decision (ADR-0005, tracked). No fix is required.
>
> Incidental and outside this finding's scope (not part of the refutation): ADR-0005 §Decision states the lockfile is "1,467 lines"; `git show HEAD:uv.lock | wc -l` reports 1473.

`reproduction` block, lifted from the refutation evidence:

```yaml
reproduction:
  command: "git ls-files -s uv.lock; git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock; git log --oneline --diff-filter=A -- uv.lock; git diff HEAD --stat -- uv.lock; git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all"
  observed: "`git ls-files -s uv.lock` returns `100644 3f3e2b1b5cc12bf4213b20b3069a75ed55e6fd7e 0\\tuv.lock`, satisfying the deliverable spec's Thread B item 1 check verbatim. `git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock` succeeds, so the file is in the HEAD tree and therefore in every clone. The adding commit is `2ba291f build: track uv.lock as the pinning mechanism; ADR-0005; declare s4-reexecution session`, one commit behind HEAD `01ecfe7`. `git diff HEAD --stat -- uv.lock` is empty, so the committed blob equals the working tree, and `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` does not list uv.lock. The auditor's `?? uv.lock` came from the session-start gitStatus snapshot at HEAD `dd4d151`, which predates commit 2ba291f. Both halves of Thread B item 1 shipped."
```

### REV-1-2 — retained

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** verification-gap
> **location:** docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-rule.md:3-10
> **issue:** The claim that the subset rule was fixed BEFORE assessment — the load-bearing pre-registration claim of the whole stage — rests entirely on assertions made inside the artifact whose priority is in question, plus a digest carried forward into files written afterwards. The timestamp and the 'Git HEAD at fixing' are self-declared in the rule file itself; the SHA-256 chain into ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl's `_header` and into A16 establishes only that the verdict file was written after some version of the rule file, not that the rule file preceded the reading. Nothing external timestamps it. This project already owns the correct mechanism and used it for the parent artifact: the frozen protocol was registered by commit 27d74738aa35ec1cdf1ec6915b50532e3620ea6f BEFORE any query executed, and the review's `registration` field says so in terms. That discipline was not applied to the subset rule, and no commit carrying the rule file predates the assessment. The session's own standing correction forbids exactly this ('No deliverable here is reported complete on a remediating agent's own account of its work'), and the rule file's disclosed prior exposure of U00024 shows the author understood the priority question was live.
> **evidence:** "**Status of this file:** written and hashed **before any X11 record was read for assessment**. It is cited by its SHA-256 in the protocol amendment that declares the stage and in the `_header` of `ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl`." — with the only supporting facts being "**Fixed at:** 2026-09-03T17:51:21Z (UTC, `date -u` at the moment of writing)" and "**Git HEAD at fixing:** `2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433`", both written by the same agent in the same file.
> **fix:** State in the rule file and in A16 that the pre-fixing is SELF-ATTESTED and carries no external timestamp, and name what would have established it. For any future stage of this kind, commit the rule file (or a commit trailer carrying its digest) before the assessing agent is dispatched, exactly as commit 27d7473 registered the protocol before any query ran; then cite that commit, not a `date -u` string, as the priority evidence.
> **reference:** CHAMP item 6 (consistency with the pre-registered protocol / frozen design); review frontmatter `registration:` field, which sets the project's own standard for what a registration event is

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> No refutation found; the finding reproduces exactly. (1) Quoted text at ka-s4-subset-rule.md:3-10 is verbatim, and the only priority evidence in the file is the self-written `date -u` string (2026-09-03T17:51:21Z) and a self-written `Git HEAD at fixing` value. (2) `git log --all -- docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-rule.md` returns zero commits; `git status --short` lists the file as untracked (`??`). No commit carries the rule file at any point, so a fortiori none predates the assessment. (3) Full messages of both Sep-3 commits were read: 2ba291f (2026-09-03T12:47:37-05:00) and 01ecfe7 (13:03:30-05:00) contain no reference to the rule digest and no trailer carrying it; their only provenance trailers point to repro_log_89e21c193ba8414a8628283dcff23c86.json, whose mtime is Sep 2 16:32, predating the stage. (4) Repo-wide grep for the rule digest 5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6 matches only four files — ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl, ka-s4-subset-membership.json, ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl, and protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md (A16 at lines 2058 and 2244) — all untracked or uncommitted working-tree writes by the same agent and all downstream of the rule, so the SHA chain establishes only relative ordering among agent-written files, precisely as the finding claims. (5) No ReproLog exists for this stage; the newest file in logs/reproducibility/ is dated Sep 2 16:32, so no independent reproducibility envelope timestamps the rule either. (6) Filesystem mtimes order consistently (rule 12:53 local, completion screen 13:51, membership 14:28) but are agent-writable, non-cryptographic, and say nothing about when the first X11 record was read; the rule file's mtime is also ~2 minutes AFTER its own declared freeze instant (17:51:21Z = 12:51:21-05:00), which weakens rather than corroborates the attestation. (7) The comparator the finding cites is real and verified: lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md line 27 `registration:` names commit 27d74738aa35ec1cdf1ec6915b50532e3620ea6f as the registration event because it "committed the frozen protocol before any query executed" — the project's own standard, not applied to the subset rule. The only external bound obtainable is a lower bound (the declared HEAD 2ba291f did not exist before 12:47:37-05:00), which does not establish priority over the reading. Defect claim stands.

### REV-1-3 — retained

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** consistency
> **location:** docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2358 (A17 §(e)); docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:3048-3049; docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:749-754
> **issue:** A third false claim in this session's amendment prose, of the same class as the 8,663/7,418 and the 88/30 coverage errors the session caught internally. Three tracked artifacts assert that FOUR transfer determinations changed at full text; only three did. The enumerations themselves show it: Glosten & Milgrom `not addressed` -> `addressed`, Kyle `not addressed` -> `explicitly excluded`, Avellaneda & Stoikov `not addressed` -> `explicitly excluded`, and Gueant et al. `explicitly excluded` **confirmed** — a confirmation is not a move. The agenda's own sentence then contradicts its own first clause two lines later ('Three of the four moved'), and §8.4's caveat block does the same ('three of the four changed *against* the corpus's prior reading'). The error inflates the reported yield of the session's headline extraction stage by one, and it now sits in an append-only addendum where it cannot be silently edited.
> **evidence:** A17 §(e): "Four E10 transfer statuses moved, and each moved on a sentence the authors wrote:" — followed by three moves and, as the fourth bullet, "**Othman et al. 2013** and **Abernethy/Chen/Vaughan 2013** keep `addressed`". Agenda: "**Four transfer determinations in the corpus's section 8.4 changed on the strength of sentences the authors wrote.** Kyle and Avellaneda & Stoikov moved from `not addressed` to `explicitly excluded`; Glosten & Milgrom moved from `not addressed` to `addressed`; Gueant et al.'s `explicitly excluded` was confirmed at full text and extended. **Three of the four moved against the corpus's prior reading**".
> **fix:** Restate as 'three transfer determinations changed and one was confirmed at full text', at all three sites. Because A17 is inside the append-only addendum, correct it by a new numbered amendment quoting the false sentence and striking it — the mechanism A13/A14/A15 already use for exactly this — rather than by editing A17 in place. Correct the review §8.4 caveat block and the agenda summary bullet directly, since neither is append-only.
> **reference:** CHAMP items 21 and 27 (no selective/incorrect reporting of what the analysis produced; interpretation limited to what the evidence supports); the session's own Standing correction, deliverable spec lines 325-337

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Refutation attempted and failed; the finding is confirmed by a diff of pre- and post-amendment transfer statuses. Every other candidate is explicitly a non-change - Feys 2026 (line 2919) states `not addressed` and that "the full text does not license changing it in either direction" plus "The 2026-09-02 record did not overstate this entry"; Chakraborty & Kearns retains the `not addressed` label; Othman et al. 2013 and Abernethy/Chen/Vaughan 2013 keep `addressed` (A17's own fourth bullet says so); the bookmaker trio keeps `addressed`. ### REV-1-4 — retained

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** consistency
> **location:** docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2275-2293 vs 2302-2309 and 2918-2932
> **issue:** The rewritten central-S4-finding block contradicts §8.4.3 and its own concluding sentence on two points, both at [FT] depth in a section whose declared purpose is depth honesty. (i) It calls Kyle's normality, Avellaneda & Stoikov's arithmetic Brownian mid-price and Gueant et al.'s Brownian reference price 'a payoff-support assumption', while the corrected finding one paragraph later correctly says the lineage 'states applicability conditions that are about price processes rather than payoffs'. A reference-price process is not a payoff support, and the corpus's own transfer clause turns on the distinction. (ii) It classifies Feys 2026 under 'Two of the six state no payoff support at all' and says he 'says nothing about boundedness or endpoint settlement', while §8.4.3's Feys entry records that the mid-price is 'stopped at a level M for the technical conditions' and that 'liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth is bounded, which the author states is "the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies"'. A stated bounding regime is not 'nothing about boundedness'. The transfer verdict is unchanged in both cases, so the conclusion survives, but the summary a reader is most likely to carry away overstates what the full texts showed.
> **evidence:** §8.4 central block: "Four of the six state a payoff-support assumption explicitly" and "**Feys 2026** assumes a continuous semimartingale mid-price and says nothing about boundedness or endpoint settlement. **[FT]**" — against §8.4.3: "The mid-price is a continuous semimartingale with a driftless-Brownian benchmark, stopped at a level M for the technical conditions; liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth is bounded, which the author states is *\"the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies\"*" — and against the corrected finding: "the inventory-risk lineage states applicability conditions that are about price processes rather than payoffs".
> **fix:** In the central block, replace 'state a payoff-support assumption explicitly' with 'state a support or process assumption explicitly', and split the four into the one payoff-support statement (Glosten & Milgrom's V >= 0, var(V) < inf) and the three price-process statements, which is what the corrected sentence already does. For Feys, replace 'says nothing about boundedness or endpoint settlement' with the §8.4.3 wording — a stopping level M and a bounded liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth are stated, but no payoff support for the traded claim and no endpoint settlement — keeping the `not addressed` verdict.
> **reference:** CHAMP items 21, 24 and 30 (results presented consistently; interpretation follows from the evidence; conclusions limited to what the analysis supports); internal consistency between a section's summary and its own detail

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Attempted refutation failed; both defect claims reproduce verbatim at the stated locations in C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md.
>
> (ii) is fully confirmed and is alone sufficient to sustain the finding. Central block, lines 2291-2293: "**Feys 2026** assumes a continuous semimartingale mid-price and says nothing about boundedness or endpoint settlement. **[FT]**" — placed under the line-2288 heading "**Two of the six state no payoff support at all**". Section 8.4.3 Feys entry, lines 2920-2923: "The mid-price is a continuous semimartingale with a driftless-Brownian benchmark, stopped at a level M for the technical conditions; liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth is bounded, which the author states is *\"the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies\"*". Two stated boundedness conditions (a stopping level M on the price process, and bounded liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth explicitly flagged by the author as the applicability regime) directly contradict "says nothing about boundedness". No generous reading rescues it: the central-block sentence is unqualified. The transfer verdict `not addressed` is indeed identical in both places, so the finding's own statement that the conclusion survives is also accurate.
>
> (i) is confirmed against the stated internal-contradiction target. Line 2275 labels all four items "a payoff-support assumption explicitly", enumerating Kyle's normal liquidation value, Avellaneda & Stoikov's driftless arithmetic Brownian mid-price, Guéant et al.'s Brownian reference price, and Glosten & Milgrom's V >= 0 / var(V) < inf. The concluding sentence eighteen lines later (2304-2306) says of the same lineage: "the inventory-risk lineage states applicability conditions that are about price processes rather than payoffs". Same records, incompatible category labels, one paragraph apart.
>
> One partial counter-observation I obtained, which does NOT refute the finding: section 8.4.3 uses the same "payoff support" phrasing for two of the three contested records — line 2857-2858 "The record states its payoff support on p. 1317" (Kyle) and line 2879 "the full text states the payoff support in section 2.1" (Avellaneda & Stoikov). So the central block is terminologically consistent with 8.4.3 on that word, and the sharper defect is central block vs. its own concluding sentence rather than vs. 8.4.3. The document elsewhere treats the two as distinct fields (line 2813 lists "the payoff support assumed by the model, every applicability condition" as separate extraction items; line 1070's X6 rule turns on "neither payoff support nor an applicability condition"), so the inconsistency is real under the corpus's own vocabulary. This narrows one clause of the finding's framing; it leaves the defect claim standing.

### REV-1-5 — retained

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **reproduced-check**; reproduction_required: **true**; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** verification-gap
> **location:** docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md:6-8 and 34
> **issue:** ADR-0005 over-claims in its own header and supplies no verifiable evidence for its central factual assertion. The header block states the ADR 'Closes verification gap VG-14', while §'Why this does not fully close VG-14' — three sections later, with the explicit warning that 'the gap is easy to over-claim as closed' — says it does not. A reader who reads only the status block gets the claim the body exists to prevent. Separately, the Decision asserts '`uv.lock` is committed' in the present tense as an accomplished fact, but names no commit, and the repository state visible to this audit shows `?? uv.lock` — untracked. The deliverable spec's own check for this item is `git ls-files uv.lock` returns the path; the ADR is the artifact that is supposed to record that it does, and it records nothing that can be checked from a clone. This is the same defect class the ADR itself identifies in the `pip_freeze_sha256` field: 'a digest whose referent is unreachable from a clone pins nothing'.
> **evidence:** Header: "**Supersedes:** none. Closes verification gap VG-14 recorded in [lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md](../literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md) §12.3." — against "### Why this does not fully close VG-14 / Stated plainly, because the gap is easy to over-claim as closed". Decision: "`uv.lock` is committed. It resolves the full dependency graph — 1,467 lines". Repository state at audit: "?? uv.lock".
> **fix:** Change the header to 'Partially closes VG-14; see §Why this does not fully close VG-14', so the status block and the body agree. Add the commit hash that tracks `uv.lock` to the Decision section (and the lockfile's SHA-256), and verify `git ls-files uv.lock` before ticking the spec box; if the file is not yet tracked, say so and tick the box only after it is. Record the residual VG-14 half explicitly in the review's §12.3 gap table rather than only in this ADR.
> **reference:** CHAMP item 29 (conclusions limited to what the work supports); deliverable spec Thread B item 1 check; CLAUDE.md §Reproducibility contract (clone-durable carriers)

Refutation (counter-evidence, `reproduced-check`):

> NOT REFUTED overall. The finding is compound; one limb is refuted with concrete counter-evidence, the core limb is confirmed by reproduction, so the finding stands in narrowed form.
>
> CONFIRMED (limb 1, the header over-claim — this is the defect that survives). Reproduced in the COMMITTED version, not merely the worktree: `git show HEAD:docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md` line 6 reads "- **Supersedes:** none. Closes verification gap VG-14 recorded in ... §12.3.", while line 59 heads "### Why this does not fully close VG-14" and line 61 reads "Stated plainly, because the gap is easy to over-claim as closed:". `git diff --stat HEAD` on the file is empty, so worktree and HEAD agree. The status block and the body contradict each other, and a reader stopping at the header gets exactly the claim the body was written to prevent. Finding's characterization of this limb is accurate at the stated location (lines 6-8 vs 59).
>
> REFUTED (limb 2, the "`uv.lock` is untracked / records nothing checkable from a clone" assertion). Counter-checks run in the live repository:
> - `git ls-files uv.lock` -> returns `uv.lock` (exit 0). This is verbatim the deliverable spec's Thread B item 1 check ("check: `git ls-files uv.lock` returns the path"), quoted at line 82 of docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md. It passes.
> - `git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock` -> succeeds; the file is in the HEAD tree, therefore in every clone.
> - `git log -1 --format=%H -- uv.lock` -> most recent touch 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b; introduced in 2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433 "build: track uv.lock as the pinning mechanism; ADR-0005; declare s4-reexecution session".
> - `git show --name-only 2ba291f` file list: `.gitattributes`, `docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md`, `docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md`, `uv.lock`. The ADR and the lockfile were committed TOGETHER, so the sub-claim "names no commit" describes an impossibility rather than an omission: an ADR cannot carry the hash of the commit that introduces it. That commit also adds `uv.lock text eol=lf` to `.gitattributes`, making the checkout form (and hence any digest over it) platform-stable.
> - The auditor's quoted repository state "?? uv.lock" is the session-start gitStatus snapshot embedded in context, which predates commit 2ba291f. It is stale, not the state at audit time. The Decision's present-tense "`uv.lock` is committed" is therefore TRUE as written, and the parallel drawn to the `pip_freeze_sha256` defect ("a digest whose referent is unreachable from a clone pins nothing") does not hold: the lockfile's referent — 1,467 lines with per-package sdist/wheel SHA-256 — is IN the clone, not behind a gitignored path.
>
> Net effect on remediation scope: the ADR header/body contradiction is real and needs the wording fix. The "add the commit hash / verify git ls-files before ticking the box / the file is not yet tracked" half of the claimed fix is moot — the file is tracked and the check passes. One adjacent fact, outside this finding's stated locations but bearing on its third sub-claim: lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md line 3580 (§12.3 VG-14 row) still asserts "the only lock artifact, `uv.lock`, is untracked (`git ls-files uv.lock` returns nothing)", and line 4170 repeats it. Those are now stale in the opposite direction from the finding's charge — the review under-claims where the ADR over-claims.

`reproduction` block, lifted from the refutation evidence:

```yaml
reproduction:
  command: "git show HEAD:docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md | sed -n '6p;59p;61p'; git diff --stat HEAD -- docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md; git ls-files uv.lock; git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock; git log -1 --format=%H -- uv.lock; git show --name-only 2ba291f"
  observed: "In the COMMITTED version, line 6 reads \"- **Supersedes:** none. Closes verification gap VG-14 recorded in ... \u00a712.3.\" while line 59 heads \"### Why this does not fully close VG-14\" and line 61 reads \"Stated plainly, because the gap is easy to over-claim as closed:\"; `git diff --stat HEAD` on the file is empty, so worktree and HEAD agree. The header/body contradiction is therefore real in the tracked record (limb 1 CONFIRMED). Against limb 2: `git ls-files uv.lock` returns `uv.lock`, `git cat-file -e HEAD:uv.lock` succeeds, `git log -1 --format=%H -- uv.lock` gives 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b with the file introduced by 2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433, whose file list is `.gitattributes`, the ADR, the deliverable spec and `uv.lock` \u2014 the ADR and the lockfile were committed together, so the ADR cannot carry the hash of the commit that introduces it, and the present-tense \"`uv.lock` is committed\" is true. Limb 2 refuted; the finding stands in narrowed form."
```

### REV-1-6 — retained

- raised_by: critical-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** standard-coverage
> **location:** docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:287-297 (F-1); docs/methodology/protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:885-899 (A8)
> **issue:** F-1 is correctly identified and the diagnosis is confirmed mechanically by tests/test_erob_recount.py, which reproduces A8's 31/13 exactly against the review's §2.8-RESOLVED domain column while the raw `domain_concerns` field gives 42. But the stated reason for not repairing it — 'correcting a published amendment in an append-only addendum is a substantive act on a frozen protocol, and the lead session should not do it on its own reading' — conflates editing an amendment with appending a correcting one, and the same addendum already establishes the second is available: A12 corrects A7's answered-cell denominator from 585 to 561 by append, and the test's own docstring relies on that ('the addendum is append-only, so A12 is the live figure and is what this reads'). The kalshi addendum does the same thing three times over (A13, A14, A15 all strike prior amendment assertions by quotation). The remedy was therefore in hand and was not used, and the consequence is that a frozen governing document still carries a false statement about the provenance of its own headline count, with the correction living only in a session deliverable spec that no consumer of the protocol reads.
> **evidence:** A8: "The delivered appraisal records domain concerns as the primary extractor's own judgments, not as mechanical derivations from the Q cells ... Recount over the 72 appraisal rows with §6's domain-to-question map ...: **31 domain cells are recorded `low`**" — against spec F-1: "They are counted against the review's §2.8-**resolved** domain column, i.e. after the `†` raises. Recount against the raw `domain_concerns` field in `se-extraction-primary.jsonl` gives **42** ... **Not repaired:** correcting a published amendment in an append-only addendum is a substantive act on a frozen protocol".
> **fix:** Append a numbered amendment to the explosive-regime protocol addendum that quotes A8's sentence, states that the 31 and the 13 are counted against the review's §2.8-resolved domain column rather than the primary extractor's raw `domain_concerns` field, gives the raw-field recount (42; D1 4, D2 1, D3 21, D4 9, D5 6, D6 1) and cites tests/test_erob_recount.py as the mechanical ground. Do not edit A8. Cite A12's correction of A7 as the precedent so the append is visibly the sanctioned mechanism and not a fresh licence.
> **reference:** CHAMP item 6 (consistency with the frozen design) and item 20 (data described correctly); the addendum's own append-only correction precedent (A12 correcting A7)

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> No refutation. Every element reproduced. (1) A8 at protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:885-899 reads verbatim as quoted, asserting the appraisal "records domain concerns as the primary extractor's own judgments" and then reporting 31 lenient / 13 strict. (2) Spec F-1 at deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:287-297 reads verbatim as quoted, including the non-repair reason. (3) `uv run pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py -q` -> 9 passed; it derives 31/13 against the review's published SS6 domain column. (4) My own independent recount (script written for this gate, not the test): applying SS6's Q->D map with SS2.8-resolved Q answers against the RAW `domain_concerns` field of se-extraction-primary.jsonl yields lenient 42 (D1 4, D2 1, D3 21, D4 9, D5 6, D6 1) and strict 13 (D3 1, D4 7, D5 5) - exactly the spec's figures and exactly A8's strict split; the raw field never yields 31 (primary-only Q answers give 32/15). (5) Parsing the review's SS6 table, 29 published D cells differ from the raw field and 27 carry the dagger SS2.8-raise marker, so the published column and the extractor's raw judgments are demonstrably different objects. (6) The append-correction precedent is real and explicit: A12 states "SS10 makes this addendum append-only, so A7 and A11 are not edited; they are corrected here" and corrects 585 -> 561; A14 does the same over A13(b) ("A13's text is not edited because the addendum is append-only"). (7) No correction of A8 exists anywhere tracked: the string `domain_concerns` occurs in no file except the session spec, and neither the protocol nor the review carries the raw-field recount. Only mitigating observation, which is a reasoning quibble and not counter-evidence: A12 and A14 are both headed "round-2 / round-3 correcting entry", i.e. the append precedent was exercised under audit-round authority, and the spec does route F-1 "to the audit round" - which bears on whether the lead session's deferral was unreasonable, not on whether the defect exists.

### CODE-1-1 — retained

- raised_by: code-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **reproduced-check**; reproduction_required: **true**; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** tests
> **location:** tests/test_erob_recount.py:678-707 (test_no_free_numeric_constants)
> **issue:** The module's no-magic-number guard only walks `ast.Constant` nodes of type int/float, so any published figure written inside a *string* literal (notably a regex anchor) passes it undetected. It also allow-lists by VALUE across the whole file rather than per-site, so once a value is on the list it may appear anywhere for any purpose. The guard therefore does not enforce the property its docstring asserts ("Any published figure ... is far outside that set, so hardcoding one fails here") — and a published figure is in fact hardcoded (see CODE-1-2).
> **evidence:** literals = {node.value for node in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, int | float) ...}; assert literals <= structural_indices
> **fix:** Extend the walk to string constants: for every `ast.Constant` of type `str`, scan with `re.findall(r"(?<![A-Za-z_])\\d+", value)` and require each captured integer to be either a regex-structural quantifier or a member of the allow-list; exclude `\\d`/`\\w` escapes and character-class bounds explicitly. Optionally attach the allow-list to (value, lineno) pairs so each index is justified at its site rather than globally.
> **reference:** https://peps.python.org/pep-0008/ ; CLAUDE.md "Parameter & Prompt Selection"

Refutation (counter-evidence, `reproduced-check`):

> Refutation attempt failed — all three components of the finding reproduce at C:\Users\skoir\castles\tests\test_erob_recount.py:678-707.
>
> (1) Evidence text verified verbatim. Lines 698-707 read: `literals = {node.value for node in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, int | float) and not isinstance(node.value, bool)}` then `assert literals <= structural_indices`. The walk is filtered to int/float Constants; `ast.Constant` nodes of type `str` are never inspected. Confirmed by re-running the same comprehension out-of-band: the numeric-literal set is exactly {0, 1, 2, 7, 9, 10} and is a subset of `structural_indices`, so the assert passes.
>
> (2) The docstring property is not enforced, and a named counterexample is live in the file. The guard's own docstring (line 685-687) lists "50 `partial` cells" among the published figures that "far outside that set, so hardcoding one fails here". Line 252 hardcodes exactly that figure inside a string literal used as an executable regex anchor:
>     denominator = _require(r"\*\*50 of (\d+) = ", a12, "A12's corrected answered-cell denominator")
> `50` here is the published `partial`-cell count — protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:1033 states "**50 of 561 = 8.9%**". The neighbouring denominator on the same line is captured as `(\d+)` rather than typed, which is the module's stated convention; the numerator is typed. Ran `uv run pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py::test_no_free_numeric_constants -q` against the file as shipped: "1 passed in 0.02s". The guard passes while a figure its docstring names as a guaranteed failure is hardcoded in the module.
>
> (3) Allow-list scoping claim also holds by inspection: `literals` is a set of bare values with no `lineno` carried, and the comparison is `literals <= structural_indices` over the whole file, so any value once allow-listed is accepted at every site for any purpose. There is no per-site justification mechanism.
>
> Also ran an independent AST scan of every `str` Constant in the module for embedded integers outside the allow-list. Beyond docstring prose (lines 245, 604, 679) and filename/section-heading tokens, the one load-bearing executable hit is line 252. That single hit is sufficient: it is code whose matching behaviour depends on the hardcoded published figure, and the guard does not see it.
>
> No counter-evidence found. Note this is scoped to the defect claim only; I did not assess whether the proposed regex-based fix is the right remedy.

`reproduction` block, lifted from the refutation evidence:

```yaml
reproduction:
  command: "uv run pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py::test_no_free_numeric_constants -q; python - <<'PY'  # re-run the guard's own AST comprehension out-of-band, then scan every str Constant for embedded integers\nPY"
  observed: "\"1 passed in 0.02s\" against the file as shipped. The guard's own comprehension returns the numeric-literal set {0, 1, 2, 7, 9, 10}, a subset of `structural_indices`, so the assert passes. The independent scan of `ast.Constant` string values finds one load-bearing executable hit at line 252, `denominator = _require(r\"\\\\*\\\\*50 of (\\\\d+) = \", a12, \"A12's corrected answered-cell denominator\")`, hardcoding the published `partial`-cell count 50 that the guard's own docstring names as a figure whose hardcoding \"fails here\" (protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:1033 states \"**50 of 561 = 8.9%**\"). The guard passes while the figure it names is hardcoded, so the docstring property is not enforced."
```

### CODE-1-2 — retained

- raised_by: code-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** design
> **location:** tests/test_erob_recount.py:252 (read_a12_partial_accounting)
> **issue:** A published figure — A12's 50-cell `partial` numerator — is hardcoded inside the regex used to read A12's denominator, contradicting the module docstring's absolute claim "Every scalar this module compares against is parsed ... There are no free constants". Worse, it is an *anchor* rather than a capture: the derived numerator (`sum(by_question.values())`) is never compared to the amendment's stated numerator, and if A12 were corrected to a different numerator the regex would simply not match, surfacing as "could not read A12's corrected answered-cell denominator" — an input-parse error masquerading as a document-shape problem instead of the divergence it actually is.
> **evidence:** denominator = _require(r"\\*\\*50 of (\\d+) = ", a12, "A12's corrected answered-cell denominator")  # A12 text: "**50 of 561 = 8.9%**"
> **fix:** Capture both sides: `_require(r"\\*\\*(\\d+) of (\\d+) = ", a12, ...)`, return the numerator as `stated_partial_total`, and add `assert accounting['total'] == accounting['stated_partial_total']` in `test_partial_extension_matches_amendments_a7_and_a12` so a numerator change fails as a divergence with a readable message. Apply the same treatment to the loose `r"(\\d+) . 7 \\(Q1-Q7\\) \\+ (\\d+) . 2 \\(Q8, Q9\\)"` anchor at line 254, whose `.` also silently matches any character where `×` is intended.
> **reference:** https://peps.python.org/pep-0257/

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> No refutation found; every element of the claim reproduced. (1) tests/test_erob_recount.py:252 is verbatim `denominator = _require(r"\*\*50 of (\d+) = ", a12, "A12's corrected answered-cell denominator")`, and the A12 source text at docs/methodology/protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:1033 is `**50 of 561 = 8.9%**` — the numerator 50 is an uncaptured anchor. (2) Counter-test: baseline `uv run pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py -q` gives 9 passed; mutating the protocol text 50->51 and calling read_a12_partial_accounting raises `could not read A12's corrected answered-cell denominator from its source document`, i.e. a parse-shape error rather than a numerator divergence, exactly as claimed. (3) Reproducing the AST scan used by test_no_free_numeric_constants returns int literals [0, 1, 2, 7, 9, 10]; 50 is not among them because it is inside a string literal, so that test's own docstring claim that hardcoding "50 `partial` cells" "fails here", and the module docstring's "There are no free constants", are both contradicted. (4) The derived numerator is genuinely never compared to A12's stated one: accounting["total"] is sum(by_question.values()) over the parsed per-question split, so line 594 compares derived-vs-split, not derived-vs-stated-50. (5) The secondary claim about line 254 also holds: r"(\d+) . 7 \(Q1-Q7\) \+ (\d+) . 2 \(Q8, Q9\)" matches "65 Z 7 (Q1-Q7) + 53 Q 2 (Q8, Q9)", so the `.` where `×` is intended accepts any character. Only defensible counter-argument is a narrow reading that 50 is not a scalar "compared against" — that is a wording quibble, not counter-evidence, and it does not survive test_no_free_numeric_constants' explicit naming of 50.

### CODE-1-3 — retained

- raised_by: code-reviewer; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** types
> **location:** tests/test_erob_recount.py:310-338 (load_primary)
> **issue:** Incorrect return annotation. `load_primary` is declared `-> dict[str, dict]` but two of its four keys hold `set[str]` (`unappraised`, `twins`). Consumers rely on the set behaviour (`primary["unappraised"] - rechecked`, `len(primary["twins"])`), which the declared type forbids. Any type checker run over `tests/` reports this; the annotation is actively misleading rather than merely absent.
> **evidence:** def load_primary(path: Path) -> dict[str, dict]:  ...  return {"answers": answers, "recorded_domains": recorded, "unappraised": unappraised, "twins": twins}  # unappraised/twins are set[str]
> **fix:** Declare a `TypedDict` (e.g. `class PrimaryPass(TypedDict): answers: dict[str, dict[str, str]]; recorded_domains: dict[str, dict[str, str]]; unappraised: set[str]; twins: set[str]`) or a frozen dataclass, and annotate the return with it. Do the same for `read_a8_divergence_enumeration` and `read_a12_partial_accounting`, whose `dict[str, Counter | int]` returns force every consumer to use an un-narrowed union in int- and Counter-specific contexts.
> **reference:** https://peps.python.org/pep-0484/

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Refutation attempt failed; the finding is confirmed. (1) Source text at C:\Users\skoir\castles\tests\test_erob_recount.py:310 is verbatim `def load_primary(path: Path) -> dict[str, dict]:`, with local bindings `unappraised: set[str] = set()` (line 319) and `twins: set[str] = set()` (line 320) returned under keys "unappraised" (line 336) and "twins" (line 337). (2) Counter-test run: `uv run --with mypy mypy tests/test_erob_recount.py` (mypy 2.3.1) emits exactly the claimed error, inside the cited 310-338 span: `tests\test_erob_recount.py:336: error: Dict entry 2 has incompatible type "str": "set[str]"; expected "str": "dict[Any, Any]"  [dict-item]` and the same at line 337 for entry 3. So "any type checker run over tests/ reports this" holds against a real checker, not just in principle. (3) Set-dependent consumers confirmed: line 540 `no_fulltext = primary["unappraised"] - rechecked`, line 542 `assert len(primary["twins"]) == shape["twins"]`, line 546 `classes = [appraised | rechecked, no_fulltext, primary["twins"]]` followed by `set().union(*classes)` — all operations the declared `dict` value type forbids. (4) The two secondary annotations named in the fix also exist as described: line 212 `def read_a8_divergence_enumeration(protocol_text: str) -> dict[str, Counter | int]:` and line 244 `def read_a12_partial_accounting(protocol_text: str) -> dict[str, Counter | int]:`, both returning mixed int / Counter values. No TypedDict or typing import narrows any of these. The only angle that could soften the finding is that the consuming pytest fixture is annotated bare `-> dict` (line 480), so mypy does not additionally flag lines 540-546 — but that masks the error at the call sites rather than making the declaration correct, and does not touch the two confirmed errors at the return statement itself. Nothing here disproves the claim. Incidental: mypy also flags an unrelated line 437 `arg-type` error not covered by this finding.

### QUANT-1-1 — retained

- raised_by: quant-auditor; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** method
> **location:** docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl:2-198; docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2156-2171 (A16 §d)
> **issue:** The 178 `include` verdicts are not separately evidenced against the criterion that decides them. A16 §(d) fixes the include bar as "the abstract itself states the model's assumed environment ... or a condition under which its result holds", and explicitly rules that "a statement only of the record's topic or method was not counted as either". No field on any include row records that statement. `criterion_cited` is byte-identical boilerplate on 177 of 178 rows, and `rationale` is the SR-1 subset-membership rationale (does the record specify a model of an S4 object) — the object half of C3, not the reserved second conjunct. The eligibility test the subset rule promised to apply *inside* the subset therefore leaves no per-record trace, and the include verdict collapses onto subset membership: 178 of 197 = 90.4% include, with the only exclusions coming from a code (X5) that turns on the instrument, not on the conjunct.
> **evidence:** `collections.Counter(r['criterion_cited'] for r in includes)` returns exactly two values: 177 × "I1 via the section 2.2 transfer clause; I2 via C3; I3 satisfied by construction of the X11 stratum ...; I4 satisfied at abstract depth" and 1 × the U01611 direct-I1 string. Rationales that are topic/method descriptions and carry no environment or condition, yet are `include`: U04335 — "Enriches the market maker's strategy space with the option not to quote or to quote single-sided, and compares performance against continuously quoting makers in adversarial environments"; U00074 — "Specifies market-maker strategies in a simulated continuous double auction with private and common values and evaluates their welfare effect; the market maker is the modelled object"; U00224 — "Market makers compete in nonlinear tariffs against a privately informed insider; the market-maker pricing schedule is the modelled object". Contrast the one row that does evidence it: U01376 — "the record states the conditions (absence of adverse selection, profit volatility) under which its results hold".
> **fix:** Add a mandatory `admitting_statement` field to every include row quoting the sentence from the record's own retrievable text that carries either the payoff support or the applicability condition, with its source (abstract / recovered abstract / title). Re-adjudicate to `undecided` every row for which no such sentence can be quoted, and restate the include count and every downstream count that uses 327.
> **reference:** Frozen protocol §2.2 transfer clause and §2.6 J4 ("state applicability conditions and the record is in ... state none and X6 applies"); PRISMA 2020 item 16b (records excluded after assessment, with reasons).

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Refutation attempt failed; the finding is confirmed and strengthened. (1) Reproduced at ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl: 197 record rows, verdicts include=178, exclude=12 (all X5/fails B-a), undecided=7; criterion_cited over the 178 includes takes exactly two values (177 x the "I1 via the section 2.2 transfer clause; I2 via C3; I3 satisfied by construction of the X11 stratum ...; I4 satisfied at abstract depth" string, 1 x the U01611 direct-I1 string); the union of row keys contains no field for an admitting statement. Quoted rationales for U04335, U00074, U00224, U01376 match. (2) Counter-test A - downstream trace: all 178 include ids appear in ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl, but each A16-added record has extraction_performed=false, E10.transfer_status="not extracted", and only screen_criterion_cited / screen_rationale, which are verbatim copies of the same two screen fields. No independent trace exists. (3) Counter-test B - the strongest available defense, that the rationale text itself states the assumed environment for most rows and the auditor cherry-picked three examples: this collapses. Diffing each completion-screen rationale against the corresponding SR-1 note in ka-s4-subset-membership.json gives 178 of 178 include rationales BYTE-IDENTICAL to the subset-membership note, while all 19 non-include rows (12 X5, 7 undecided) differ by adding verdict-specific reasoning. The include rows therefore contain no text authored for the eligibility decision at all, which proves the claimed defect more sharply than the finding states it. (4) Counter-test C - did the reserved conjunct discriminate at all: the 7 undecided rows do recite it ("states neither the payoff support ... nor a condition under which its result holds"), and under A16 section (d) the conjunct's negative outcome at abstract depth is 'undecided' rather than X6, so the finding's remark that the only exclusions come from X5 understates that one point. This qualifies a single clause and does not touch the 178 include rows. (5) Source check: ka-s4-subset-rule.md states "SR-1 uses only the first [conjunct]. The second conjunct is deliberately reserved as the eligibility test applied inside the subset"; protocol A16 section (d) contains "A statement only of the record's topic or method was not counted as either". Both support the finding as written.

### QUANT-1-2 — retained

- raised_by: quant-auditor; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** method
> **location:** docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2156-2171 (A16 §d); docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:3013-3018
> **issue:** The X6-versus-undecided reasoning is sound in one direction and is applied in one direction only, which makes the screen one-sidedly permissive. A16 §(d) correctly observes that the transfer clause's bar ("the corpus can state what the record's own text says about its payoff support AND applicability conditions") and X6's bar ("states NEITHER payoff support NOR an applicability condition") are different, then elects X6's bar. But X6 governs *exclusion*; I1 admits a non-in-scope C3 record only "under the transfer clause", whose bar is conjunctive. Electing X6's disjunctive negation as the *admission* test converts an "eligible only if A and B" into "eligible unless neither A nor B". The stated epistemic asymmetry — that an abstract can carry a positive existence claim but cannot establish a universal negative — is true, but it cuts both ways: at abstract depth the corpus equally cannot establish the conjunctive positive the transfer clause requires. The third verdict the rule file itself provides ("still undecided, inside the subset") was the available honest disposition and was used 7 times in 197. The structural consequence is that X6 — the one exclusion code the frozen protocol wrote specifically for this literature (J4) — has a guaranteed rate of zero at this stage, and the amendment nowhere states that consequence or bounds how much of the 178 it accounts for.
> **evidence:** ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl `_header.verdict_counts` = {include 178, exclude_X5 12, undecided_in_subset 7}; X6 appears in no row. A16 §(d): "A record can be included at abstract depth when the abstract itself states the model's assumed environment ... or a condition under which its result holds ... A record cannot be coded X6 at abstract depth." Frozen §2.2: "eligible only if the corpus can state what the record's own text says about its payoff support and applicability conditions" (conjunction).
> **fix:** State in A16 §(d) that the bar applied for admission is the negation of X6 and not the transfer clause's conjunction, that X6's stage rate is zero by construction of that choice, and that the include count is therefore an upper bound on the set the transfer clause admits. Report the count of includes that satisfy the conjunction (payoff support AND applicability condition both quotable) as the lower bound, so the range is visible.
> **reference:** Frozen protocol §2.2 (transfer clause), §2.3 I1, §2.4 X6, §2.6 J4.

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Refutation attempted and failed; every claimed element reproduced, and one independent check corroborates the finding further.
>
> REPRODUCED (all exact):
> 1. C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\search_logs\kalshi-arbitrage\ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl `_header.verdict_counts` = {"include": 178, "exclude_X5": 12, "undecided_in_subset": 7}; 198 lines (1 header + 197 rows). Parsed Counter over rows: verdict {include 178, exclude 12, undecided 7}; primary_code {None 178, X5 12, X11 7}. X6 is the primary_code of ZERO rows. The 13 grep hits for "X6" are all prose inside `rationale` ("X5 precedes X6 in the section 2.4 first-code-that-applies order" x12, and U00228's "X6 asserts the record states neither, which cannot be established from an abstract"). X6 stage rate is zero, as claimed.
> 2. A16 §(d) is at protocol line 2156 ("#### (d) The X6-versus-undecided reading, stated because the frozen text is not univocal"), quoted verbatim in the finding at lines 2158-2171, including "A record cannot be coded X6 at abstract depth" and the disjunctive admission sentence "...states the model's assumed environment ... or a condition under which its result holds".
> 3. Frozen §2.2 transfer clause (protocol line ~204) is conjunctive verbatim: "eligible only if the corpus can state what the record's own text says about its payoff support and applicability conditions". Frozen X6 (§2.4): "A C3 record that states neither payoff support nor an applicability condition". Frozen I1 (§2.3): "...OR is a C3 model record admitted under the transfer clause (2.2)". All as cited.
> 4. depth_read across all 197 rows is abstract or shallower (abstract 178, abstract-retrieved-at-this-stage 16, title-only 1, partial-abstract 1, truncated-abstract 1). So "X6 cannot be coded at abstract depth" does make its stage rate zero BY CONSTRUCTION for this stage, not incidentally.
> 5. No bound is reported anywhere. grep for "upper bound"/"lower bound"/"conjunct" over the protocol returns only §2.5's unrelated date-bound text and A16's restatement of the SR-1 note. `criterion_cited` is identical boilerplate on 177 of 178 includes ("I1 via the section 2.2 transfer clause; I2 via C3; I3 ...; I4 satisfied at abstract depth") and records no payoff-support/applicability-condition finding per record. The lit review at lines 3018-3019 states affirmatively "none of them has an E10, a payoff support, an applicability condition or a transfer status", i.e. the conjunction status of the 178 is unknown and unreported.
>
> INDEPENDENT CHECK THAT STRENGTHENS THE FINDING:
> C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\search_logs\kalshi-arbitrage\ka-s4-subset-rule.md (the file hashed and fixed BEFORE assessment, sha256 5f64d29b…) lines 74-85 state the design purpose explicitly: SR-1 uses only C3's first conjunct because the second "is deliberately reserved as the eligibility test applied inside the subset ... If SR-1 required both conjuncts ... no record could ever receive an X6 verdict at this stage, and the transfer clause would have no work to do. That would make the completion screen incapable of returning the one verdict the frozen protocol wrote for this literature." A16 restates this at protocol lines 2091-2095. A16 §(d) then adopts a reading that produces exactly that nullity by a different route (X6 unreachable at abstract depth, and every row is abstract depth), and nowhere notes that it defeats the stated purpose of the pre-fixed split. This is the finding's structural-consequence claim, corroborated from a source the finding did not cite.
>
> STRONGEST MITIGATION FOUND, WHICH DOES NOT REFUTE:
> Frozen §2.6 J4 (protocol lines ~311-313) operationalizes this exact class in single-predicate form: "Decided entirely by the transfer clause in 2.2 plus X6. No separate judgement is exercised: state applicability conditions and the record is in, with transfer status recorded; state none and X6 applies." So the frozen text does contain a non-conjunctive admission sentence, and A16 §(d) does disclose the non-univocality in its own heading. But J4 admits on applicability-conditions alone, whereas A16 §(d) admits on assumed-environment (payoff support) alone as well — the disjunct J4 does not license — and J4's "state none and X6 applies" is itself in conflict with X6's own "neither ... nor" text. This softens the "unlicensed conversion" framing; it does not disprove either load-bearing fact: (i) the bar actually applied for admission is X6's disjunctive negation rather than the transfer clause's conjunction, and (ii) no count or bound of conjunction-satisfying includes within the 178 exists in any artifact. Framing mitigation is not counter-evidence, so refuted=false.

### QUANT-1-3 — retained

> **location:** docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-membership.json (U01126, U02218, U03553, U03624); docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2173-2190 (A16 §e)
> **issue:** Four X11 records that could not be retrieved to abstract depth were adjudicated under SR-1/SR-2a from a title alone, contrary to SR-2d as fixed in advance, and none of the four entered the four-arm retrieval chain at all. The chain's trigger was "the archived universe carried no abstract" (129 records) rather than "no usable abstract", so records whose archived abstract field held a bibliographic citation string or a publisher correction notice were treated as retrieved and never had any arm run for them. Two of the four (U02218, U03624) were pushed out of the subset on an SR-2a ground reached from a title, a determination SR-2d forbids.
> **evidence:** membership file `depth_read` distribution: abstract 608, title-only 91, partial-abstract 1 — i.e. 92 records below abstract depth against 88 coded SR-2d. The four exceptions: U01126 (in subset, SR-1, title-only, note: "the only text retrievable for this record is a publisher correction notice"), U02218 (out, SR-2a, title-only, "the only text retrievable ... is a bibliographic citation string"), U03553 (in subset, SR-1, partial-abstract), U03624 (out, SR-2a, title-only, "the only text retrievable is a bibliographic citation string"). Checked against ka-s4-retrieval-log.json: none of the four ids appears among its 129 records, so no arm was attempted for any of them. A16 §(e) reports "88 records survived all four arms with nothing but a title" and does not disclose the additional four.
> **fix:** Run the four-arm chain for U01126, U02218, U03553 and U03624; if it returns nothing, recode all four SR-2d, correct the SR-2d count from 88 to 92 and the SR-2a residual from 259 to 257, and amend A16 §(e) to state the chain's trigger condition and that it was abstract-field presence rather than abstract-field usability.
> **reference:** ka-s4-subset-rule.md §SR-2d; frozen protocol §2.3 I4 and §2.4 X8.

(2) ka-s4-retrieval-log.json carries n_x11_without_archived_abstract=129 and 129 record entries; none of the four ids is present, so no arm was attempted for any of them. (3) Counter-test on the mechanism, run against the archived raw pulls: decoding the OpenAlex abstract_inverted_index shows all four had a NON-EMPTY archived abstract field - U01126 (ka-fc-oa-a6-p016.json) 'Abstract The above article (DOI: 10.1002/ijfe.373 ) was published online in Early View on 25 July 2008 ... the third author's surname was incorrectly spelled ...' (publisher correction notice); U02218 (ka-fc-oa-a6-p014.json) 'Ronald W. Masulis, Lakshmanan Shivakumar, Does Market Structure Affect the Immediacy of Stock Price Responses to News?, The Journal of Financial and Quantitative Analysis, Vol. 37, No. 4 (Dec., 2002), pp. 617-648' (bibliographic citation string); U03624 (ka-fc-oa-a6-p012.json) the analogous JFQA citation string; U03553 (ka-fc-oa-a6-p023.json) a single opening sentence. (4) Protocol A16 (e) states '129 of the 700' and '88 records survived all four arms with nothing but a title' and discloses no further below-abstract-depth records. (5) SR-2d in ka-s4-subset-rule.md sits in the SR-2 disjunction ('A record is OUT ... if any of the following holds'), so U01126 and U03553 being placed IN the subset under SR-1 on a title (plus one sentence for U03553) directly contradicts the rule as fixed in advance, and U02218/U03624 were adjudicated SR-2a from a title alone. > (1) Rule provenance.
