Repo-wide grep for the rule text returns only three files: the log, review §19.1 (added this round, post-retrieval), and docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md.
>
> The spec is a partial counter I tested and rejected as sufficient: `git show -s 2ba291f` gives commit time 2026-09-03 12:47:37 -0500, which precedes the log's mtime (Sep 3 13:16), and `git show 2ba291f:docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md` does state at Thread C "Every page locator and quoted string carrying a §7 synthesis claim or cited by `research_agenda_regime-classification_2026-08-21.md` is verified" — so the JSON key name is not literally the whole of the evidence for the coarse framing. But the committed spec contains NO enumeration of the six 'Synthesis claim' paragraphs, four TO COMPUTE paragraphs, §7.2-7.4 lead-ins, §7.6 three causality traps, §7.7 six pooling rows, §7.8 twelve per-detector rows, and NO exclusion rule. The membership-determining clause — "Rows of the section-7 evidence tables that no synthesis paragraph re-cites are INVENTORY, not load-bearing, and were deliberately NOT verified" — appears only in artifacts written after the retrievals. The operative boundary is therefore still unattested pre-retrieval, so the defect claim holds for the part that determines the set.
>
> (2) Missing item-level denominator. Fully confirmed. counts = {items_total 50, verified 35, corrected 7, unverifiable 8, records_in_load_bearing_set 21, records_retrieved_at_full_text 15, records_not_retrieved 6, corpus_records_read_at_full_text_across_all_rounds 14, corpus_size 72, corpus_records_never_read_at_any_verification_round 58} — no locator population. residual_statement, not_done_and_why, and review §19.1/§19.2 likewise state no candidate or inventory count; the sole occurrence of "inventory" in the JSON is the qualitative exclusion_rule string. Counter-measurement on the review's §7 span (lines 1467-2040 of docs/literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md): 76 locator-like strings (Table N x41, p.N x25, Theorem N x6, pp. x3, Figure N x1) and 30 quoted strings over 78 double-quote characters. The auditor's "78 locator-like / 33 curly-quoted" reproduces to within regex definition; the only inaccuracy is cosmetic — the file uses straight quotes (only 2 U+201C in the entire file), not curly. Candidate pool ~106 vs 50 verified, with no stated split, so the reader genuinely cannot recover the denominator.
>
> Control check on the contrast case: sha256 of docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-rule.md recomputes to 5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6, matching subset_rule_sha256 in ka-s4-subset-membership.json — Thread A's chain verifies as the auditor described, so the asymmetry is real.

### QUANT-1-6 — retained

- raised_by: quant-auditor; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** reporting
> **location:** docs/methodology/protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md (amendment A8); docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:287-297 (F-1)
> **issue:** The session's recorded finding F-1 is correct and independently reproduces, but the correction lives only in the deliverable spec, which is not the governing record. A8 describes the delivered appraisal as recording "domain concerns as the primary extractor's own judgments, not as mechanical derivations from the Q cells" and then reports 31 lenient divergences against that description. Recomputed against the raw `domain_concerns` field of se-extraction-primary.jsonl the lenient count is 42; the 31 is against the review's §2.8-resolved column. A reader of A8 — the append-only record of the appraisal's central departure — takes 31 as the rate against the extractor's own judgments, and it is 42, a 35% understatement. Reporting rather than editing the frozen amendment is the right call; leaving the correction outside every tracked governing artifact is not.
> **evidence:** Independent recount using the shipped test module's own loaders and Q→D rule: raw `domain_concerns` column → lenient 42 (D1 4, D2 1, D3 21, D4 9, D5 6, D6 1), strict 13 (D3 1, D4 7, D5 5); §2.8-resolved column → lenient 31, strict 13. A8 verbatim: "**31 domain cells are recorded `low` although a question in that domain is answered other than `yes`** — 22 of them with a `no` or `unclear` input (D3 13, D4 6, D5 2, D6 1) ... and 9 whose only non-`yes` input is `partial` (D3 8, D4 1)."
> **fix:** Append a new numbered amendment to the explosive-regime protocol addendum (strike-don't-delete) stating that A8's 31/22/9 are counted against the review's §2.8-resolved domain column, that the count against the primary extractor's raw `domain_concerns` field is 42 (D1 4, D2 1, D3 21, D4 9, D5 6, D6 1), and that the strict count is 13 under both. Mirror the note at the review's §6 legend.
> **reference:** 21 CFR 11.10(e) (append-only audit record); protocol §10 amendments policy.

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> Could not refute; the defect claim reproduces in full on independent recomputation. (1) A8 verbatim at docs/methodology/protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md:885ff reads exactly as quoted: it describes the delivered appraisal as recording domain concerns "as the primary extractor's own judgments, not as mechanical derivations from the Q cells" and, in the same paragraph, reports "**31 domain cells are recorded `low` although a question in that domain is answered other than `yes`** - 22 of them with a `no` or `unclear` input (D3 13, D4 6, D5 2, D6 1) ... and 9 whose only non-`yes` input is `partial` (D3 8, D4 1)", plus "**13 cells run the other way** ... (D3 1, D4 7, D5 5)". (2) Independent recount: I imported the shipped module's own loaders (load_primary, load_recheck, read_resolution_order, read_domain_question_map, derive_domain_rule, divergent_cells) from tests/test_erob_recount.py and ran two comparisons against the identical rule-derived column. Against the review's shipped (section-2.8-resolved) D column: lenient 31 (D3 21, D4 7, D5 2, D6 1), strict 13 (D3 1, D4 7, D5 5) - matching A8's published totals exactly. Against the raw `domain_concerns` field of se-extraction-primary.jsonl (53 appraised records carry it): lenient 42 (D1 4, D2 1, D3 21, D4 9, D5 6, D6 1), strict 13 (D3 1, D4 7, D5 5). Splitting the raw lenient set the way A8 splits its own: 33 rule-decided (`no`/`unclear` input) + 9 partial-only, versus A8's 22 + 9. So A8's 31/22 are resolved-column figures and the raw-column figures are 42/33; the strict 13 is invariant. This is exactly the finding's evidence, digit for digit. `python -m pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py -q` passes 9/9, confirming 31/22/9/13 is what the shipped table yields, i.e. the resolved column. (3) Tried and failed to find the correction in a governing artifact. The review is precise about the column's nature but never states the raw count: section 6.1(b) says "These are the primary extractor's own domain judgments **after the section 2.8 resolution**" and the section 6 legend defines the dagger on a D cell as "the domain concern is raised above the primary extractor's recorded value because of that resolution" - neither gives 42, and neither says A8's 31 is counted post-resolution. The protocol addendum is append-only and does carry later correcting entries over earlier ones (A12 corrects A7's 585 to 561 and withdraws A11's second candidate; A13/A14 correct A10(b), A9 and A13(b)), but no amendment A9-A14 touches A8's counts. A repo-wide grep for the figure in tracked prose returns the ER-RoB 42 in exactly one file, docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:287-297 (F-1); every other "42" hit is unrelated (42 executed query logs, D4 42-of-65 raised, an unrelated kalshi X5 count in the audit trail). The spec is tracked (git ls-files confirms) but is a session deliverable spec, not the protocol addendum or the review that govern the appraisal. (4) The only counter-argument available is interpretive - that A8's opening sentence characterizes the appraisal generally rather than defining the count's basis, and that its closing paragraph's "the extractor's recorded judgment" is the review-defined post-resolution column. That is a reading argument, not counter-evidence, and it does not touch the reproduced 42-vs-31 gap or the absence of any governing-record note. The finding's characterization of the gap (11/31 = 35%) is arithmetically consistent.

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

(2) `git ls-files tests/` -> tests/.gitkeep, tests/test_erob_recount.py (commit 01ecfe7), contradicting the VG-16 row at :3582, which still reads "`tests/` and `src/` contain no tracked files"; `uv run pytest tests/test_erob_recount.py -q` -> 9 passed, so a committed entrypoint does now re-derive reported numbers. (3) Line 4170 still states environment specification = "**None that resolves in a fresh clone.** ... `uv.lock` is untracked"; line 4171 still states reproduce target = "**None.** No tracked file under `tests/` or `src/` re-derives any number reported here". The document's current working-tree modification is an unrelated round-4 content pass (L-21/VG-17); all four cited lines are identical in HEAD and the working tree, so nothing pending closes them.

### LITERATURE-1-1 — retained

- raised_by: literature-check; severity_claimed: major; refuted_by: refuter (effort: high)
- evidence_type: **none**; reproduction_required: false; outcome: **retained**

Claim verbatim:

> **category:** misattributed — corpus inference presented as an author statement
> **location:** docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:270-284, :745-747
> **issue:** The session's most consequential claim — that Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle have no inventory objective, so Branch 2's H0 was vacuous for both — is asserted in the form "**Neither model has an inventory objective at all**, and both say so in their own texts" (:271), repeated in the residual as "both say so in their own texts" (:746-747). That form is correct for Glosten & Milgrom and incorrect for Kyle. The GM bullet cites a footnote that does address inventory ("if we were to recognize binding inventory constraints, we could not have a zero profit condition"). The Kyle bullet cites p.1316, which says only that prices equal the conditional expectation of the liquidation value and that market makers therefore earn zero profits on average; it says nothing about inventory. The agenda then adds "There is no inventory state variable and no reservation price" — a true observation about the model's specification, but an inference from an ABSENCE, not something Kyle states. No [corpus-inference] marker is carried on the sentence, although the same corpus's review §8.4 legend (lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2201-2204) defines that marker precisely for "a step this corpus takes from something an author states to something the author does not state", and §8.4.3's own Kyle entry (:2865-2869) is careful to mark exactly this kind of step for the payoff-support incompatibility.
> **evidence:** Kyle's cited sentence verifies verbatim against the source and contains no inventory content: "The prices determined by market makers are assumed to equal the expectation of the liquidation value of the commodity, conditional on the market makers' information sets at the dates the prices are determined" (Kyle 1985, Econometrica 53(6):1315-1335, doi:10.2307/1913210; string confirmed against the paper text via retrieval of the p.1316 passage). By contrast the GM footnote claim is corroborated independently: GM 1985 states that supporting the zero-profit condition requires assuming "the specialist has unlimited inventories of cash and securities with which to transact and the holding cost of these inventories is zero" (JFE 14(1):71-100, doi:10.1016/0304-405X(85)90044-3). So GM does address inventory in its own text; Kyle does not. The substantive conclusion is nonetheless correct — neither model carries an inventory objective — so this is a defect of attribution form, not of substance.
> **fix:** Restate at both sites as: "Neither model has an inventory objective. Glosten & Milgrom say so in their own text (footnote on binding inventory constraints and the zero-profit condition); Kyle does not discuss inventory at all — his model carries no inventory state variable and no reservation price, and that is this corpus's reading of his specification, not a statement of his. **[corpus-inference]**" Add the marker to the Kyle bullet and to the Branch 2 table's Kyle row.
> **reference:** Kyle (1985) doi:10.2307/1913210 p.1316; Glosten & Milgrom (1985) doi:10.1016/0304-405X(85)90044-3 p.76 and inventory footnote

Refutation attempted and failed (`evidence_type: none` — no counter-evidence obtained):

> No refutation obtained; the finding reproduces at every stated location. ## deferred-logged-minors

Thirty-six minors were logged and are **not** remediated this round; their full payloads are in the JSON sidecar and their compressed rows are in the findings table above. They did not pass through the refute gate, which is reserved for critical and major findings, so none of them is asserted here as established — each is a raised observation carried forward. Convergent clusters are grouped so remediation can address them once:

- **REV-1-8 / FORMAT-1-1 and REV-1-9 / FORMAT-1-2 (four-way convergent, two pairs).** The same two numeric defects in [memo_refute-gate-calibration_2026-09-03.md](../research_notes/memo_refute-gate-calibration_2026-09-03.md) were found independently by the critical-reviewer at minor severity and by the format-auditor at major severity: the §3.1 `reproduced-check` "block present" cell reading `2 of 8` against its own column total of `3 rows` and against D5's enumeration, and the §4 against-interest list naming A/2/REV-2-3 where §3.2 evidences C/2/REV-2-5. The major-severity pair (FORMAT-1-1, FORMAT-1-2) is gated and routed to remediation; the minor-severity pair is logged here so the convergence is visible and the fix is applied once.
- **CODE-1-1 / CODE-1-2 / QUANT-1-10 (three-way convergent, across two branches).** The magic-number guard's string-literal blind spot and the hardcoded `50` at [tests/test_erob_recount.py](../../tests/test_erob_recount.py):252 were found by the code-reviewer (two majors, gated and retained) and independently by the quant-auditor (QUANT-1-10, minor). QUANT-1-10 adds the executed evidence — the suite passes 9/9 with the literal present — and is logged rather than separately remediated.
- **REV-1-11 (allocation provenance) and SCOPE-1-6 (`n_residual_undecided` naming).** Both are one-sentence statements-of-fact defects in the same flow accounting: what was executed versus argued for the 138/59 stratum split, and a header field whose name asserts 503 where the review's flow uses 510. Neither changes a count.
- **SCOPE-1-3 / SCOPE-1-4 / SCOPE-1-5 (documented drift and declared scope growth).** The seven undecided subset members, the 33-of-59 full-text shortfall and the three unlisted Thread A artifacts are each declared in the delivered artifacts and are recorded here only because the spec's tick-boxes and Recorded-deviation section do not yet carry them.
- **SCOPE-1-2 (Thread E).** Sequenced after this round by construction: this trail and the provenance commit are Thread E, and the trail cannot precede the round it records. Logged so the tick is not taken as met by the presence of the separate session trail.
- **REPRODUCIBILITY-1-4 through 1-7 (repository-level reproducibility hygiene).** A dormant `data/_manifest.json` gate whose `--check` exits 0 on failure, a CRLF/LF digest hazard in the pre-`.gitattributes` corpus logs, two stale environment references, and the absence of any `uv lock --check` hook. None is currently harmful; each is a gate that cannot fire.
- **LITERATURE-1-2 through 1-7 (citation form and re-checkability).** One unverifiable quoted numeral pair, a propagated Crossref surname typo, a misdescribed withdrawal ground, one DOI cited under three years, a correction not credited to the round-3 finding that first recorded it, and the asymmetric quote-level re-checkability of publisher PDFs. No fabricated or non-resolving source was found in the session.
- **REV-1-7, REV-1-10, REV-1-12, REV-1-13, QUANT-1-7 through 1-9, QUANT-1-11, CODE-1-4 through 1-8, FORMAT-1-3 through 1-6.** Presentation, typing, error-message, link-target, naming and originality-attribution items, each cheap to fix and none bearing on a conclusion.

## residual-risk-and-sampling-caveat

**Sampling caveat.** An audit samples; the examined evidence may not be representative. Seven specialist branches read eleven artifacts against the deliverable spec, the two frozen protocols, the stored search and extraction logs, the archived pipeline scripts and a subset of the cited sources. They did not exhaustively verify every sentence, every citation-claim pairing, every extracted numeral, every screening verdict against its record, or every cross-artifact dependency. **Absence of a finding is not evidence of absence of a defect** — the same asymmetry this project's charter enforces for statistical nulls applies to this audit's own coverage. Three concrete demonstrations arose this round: the 178 include rows were checked structurally and by sample rather than record by record, so the fraction resting on a single conjunct is unknown (QUANT-1-1, QUANT-1-2); roughly a third of the session's quoted strings rest on publisher or scanned PDFs whose bytes are not retained and could not be re-executed from the audit environment (LITERATURE-1-7); and the four below-abstract-depth records in QUANT-1-3 were found only because one branch cross-tabulated `depth_read` against the SR-2d count, a check no other branch ran.

**Per-branch residual risk, as returned by each branch:**

### critical-reviewer

RQI coverage for this pass. Importance (D1): adequate, no finding — the review question is well posed, the S4 re-execution is scoped to the one strand where the 2026-09-02 record's depth claims were weakest, and the four threads are each traceable to a named recommendation. Originality (D2): covered by REV-1-13; nothing is claimed as novel, but the canonical taxonomy carrying the Branch 2/2b reorganisation is uncited. Method (D3), presentation (D4) and interpretation (D5): covered by REV-1-1 through REV-1-12.

What I could verify and what I could not. Bash is disabled in this session, so I verified everything by reading and by content search: the 138 `arms == ["forward-citation"]` rows, the 178/12/7 verdict split, the zero X6 verdicts, the five `payoff support` occurrences all falling in `undecided` rows, the 33/21/5/178 depth distribution summing to the header's 237, the seven `"verdict": "corrected"` items in the Thread C log, the presence of all four checked Thread C corrections at their review sites (en-dash forms), the agenda's Thread C advisory and informational uptake, and the memo's 136+4+11 = 151 evidence_type accounting. I could NOT execute git, so three things are asserted-not-verified by me: that `2ba291f9...` is a real commit (it matches none of the five commits in the repository snapshot available to me), that D6's 13-of-17 unrecoverable digests reproduce, and that `uv.lock` is now tracked — REV-1-2 and REV-1-5 are framed so they hold regardless of which way those resolve. I also did not run tests/test_erob_recount.py; I read it in full and it does genuinely re-derive rather than restate — every comparand is parsed from the protocol, A8, A12 or the review, `test_no_free_numeric_constants` enforces that as a property of the source file, and `test_no_divergent_cell_outside_the_marked_set` closes A8's per-domain-count shortfall against the review's own cell markers. Its self-declared blind spot (shortfall (e), the §2.8 minimum absorbing a less-concerning primary flip) is honestly bounded and I found nothing to add to it.

The residual risk that dominates. REV-1-1 is the one finding that changes what the deliverable means. If the transfer clause's conjunction is the operative inclusion bar — and the frozen I1 text says it is — then an unknown fraction of the 178 admissions rest on only one conjunct, and the 149 -> 327 headline, the 96.1%/3.9% read-based split, the 8,486/1,055 flow split and the §7 'the corpus holds 327' framing all move with it. The artifact is unusually candid about every OTHER consequence of the A16 admissions (AG-11 not papered over, the 178 carrying no claim line, the universal negatives explicitly weakened, the 503 and 545 left unassessed and named as such), which makes the silence at this one point conspicuous rather than careless: A16 §(d) sees the divergence, states it accurately, and then resolves it in the permissive direction with a one-clause justification that the review's §6, §7 and frontmatter never repeat to the reader. A second-order risk follows from the session's own diagnosed failure mode — remediation introducing false claims at a steady rate — and REV-1-3 is evidence it recurred here, a third amendment-prose error that the stage did not catch and that has now propagated into an append-only addendum. Threads B, C and D are, on my reading, materially sound: the Thread C boundary is drawn honestly and stated in three places (scope.exclusion_rule, residual_statement, not_done_and_why), the reversal of a wrong round-3 correction is recorded as such rather than presented as a fresh finding, and the memo is more careful than its own brief (it says H2-strong 'is not supported', not 'disconfirmed', and names H3 as the reading the counts actually establish). Their defects are presentation-level and cheap to fix.

### scope-auditor

Spec source selected: `docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md` (precedence 1). The frozen protocol `protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md` was consulted as a lower-precedence source; no `spec-conflict` was found between it and the declared spec — A16 §(d) records a tension internal to the frozen text (the transfer clause's "and" bar versus X6's "neither/nor" bar) and resolves it by applying X6 as written, which is a reading of the frozen protocol rather than a disagreement with the deliverable spec. Eleven spec items were enumerated (S-1..S-11). Nine are satisfied on presence: the completion-screen verdict file (197 rows, one per subset member, all carrying `criterion_cited`, header count matching A16's subset size); the pre-fixed subset rule with its digest carried into the verdict header; A16 and A17 as numbered append-only amendments; the extraction JSONL (238 lines, 237 data rows, every row carrying `depth_reached` and `extraction_performed`, 33 at `fulltext`); the in-place lit-review revision with a revision note naming this spec, restated flow, AG-11 declared and §8.4 rewritten with per-claim depth markers and the reading-list caveat partially discharged with its boundary stated; the in-place agenda rev 2 with Branch 2 supersession markers, the new Branch 2b and Branch 0's precondition restated; `tests/test_erob_recount.py` reading `se-extraction-primary.jsonl` rather than a hardcoded table; the Thread C log with its boundary declared, 50 items over 21 records at 35/7/8 and the 58-of-72 residual stated, with the pp.222-224 correction applied at all three review sites and no agenda site citing it; and the Thread D memo with the evidence_type distribution, the D1-D9 defect list including D6, the concrete diff and the eval. Two items are not met: the `uv.lock` tracking half of Thread B (SCOPE-1-1, the only major) and Thread E, which is sequenced after this round (SCOPE-1-2). Residual risk within my remit: I checked presence, absence, addition and drift only. I did not verify that the 178 includes are correct, that the read-based verdicts are genuinely read-based rather than merely asserted as such, that the GM/Kyle no-inventory-objective correction follows from the quoted passages, that the identity checks on the four wrong-paper PDF retrievals were sound, or that the test cannot pass on a mutated input — those are the method, code and reproducibility branches' calls, and a spec-to-delivery diff cannot substitute for them. The subset-size defensibility question (whether 178 is too permissive) is likewise out of this branch's scope: SR-1 was fixed before assessment and contains no numeric element, so on the presence-and-drift test the subset is the rule's consequence, not a target — whether the rule itself is too wide is a method judgement.

### code-reviewer

Only one code-bearing artifact is in the set (C:\\Users\\skoir\\castles\\tests\\test_erob_recount.py, 708 lines); the other ten artifacts are prose/JSONL and outside this branch's scope. No critical-class defects are present: no bare `except`, no mutable default arguments, no module-level side effects (all I/O is inside functions or module-scoped fixtures), no `print`-based error reporting, no PEP 8 prohibited identifiers, all imports at top, `from __future__ import annotations` present, and every function is well under 50 lines with a docstring. Ruff readiness is good against the project's own select list (E/F/W/I/N/B/UP/SIM/PL at line-length 100) — I found no line over 100 chars and no obvious PL/B/SIM violation; PLR2004 is not triggered because the string comparisons fall under ruff's default `allow-magic-value-types = [\"str\", \"bytes\"]`, which is exactly the gap CODE-1-1 describes at the project level. I did not execute the suite (AST/read-only review per agent definition), so I cannot confirm the module currently passes, cannot confirm the lead's mutation result (eru-0131 Q6 yes->no giving 31->32), and cannot confirm that the derived divergence profile in fact equals A8's counts — those are behavioural claims for the quant/reproducibility branches. The strongest code-quality risk that remains is the one CODE-1-1/CODE-1-2 name jointly: the module's central integrity claim — that it re-derives rather than restates — is enforced by a guard with a known blind spot, and there is already one published figure sitting in that blind spot. Nothing found undermines the test's basic architecture; a hardcoded shipped table could not survive `test_rule_derived_domain_column_reproduces_the_shipped_one` plus `test_resolved_answers_reproduce_the_shipped_q_cells`, both of which build their left-hand sides from the two JSONL logs.

### quant-auditor

The load-bearing verifications this round could reproduce all held — the protocol's frozen 82,677-byte prefix hashes to 99524df0…, the subset rule hashes to 5f64d29b… and its mtime/git-HEAD chain places it before the verdict file, the 138/59 stratum allocation and the whole 5,249/2,905/659 → 200/147/347/8,466 table re-derive exactly from ka-screening-verdicts.jsonl, the Glosten & Milgrom and Avellaneda & Stoikov PDFs re-fetch to the recorded byte counts and SHA-256s with every quotation and page locator verbatim (PDF page + 70 = printed page, so p.76 and p.91 are right), Kyle's file genuinely has no text layer and its JSTOR cover boilerplate matches the recorded identity string, all 59 §8.4 citation sites carry a depth marker that matches the extraction log's depth_reached with zero mismatches, D6 reproduces at exactly 4 of 17 pinned digests recoverable, and every statistic in the calibration memo (0.103, 0.057, 4.6e-21, the 9.50% one-sided bound, all five Clopper-Pearson intervals, Fisher p = 0.011 and 0.18) reproduces to the digit; what remains uncertain is whether the 178 include verdicts survive an evidenced re-adjudication under the transfer clause's conjunction rather than the negation of X6, since no per-record field records the statement that decided them and the headline n_included = 327 rests on that difference, and whether Thread C's 50-item load-bearing set is the complete extension of its own inclusion rule, which no denominator in the log lets a reader check.

### reproducibility-verifier

Verified clean, so the findings above are the exceptions rather than the pattern. Digest chain: every published SHA-256 I recomputed matches byte-exactly — subset rule 5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6 (as embedded in the completion-screen `_header`), completion screen 8e25fa35…, extraction log b7970a22… (matches frontmatter), subset membership d32dbc0d…, retrieval log dfdc6d87…, protocol frozen prefix 99524df02696… over the first 82,677 bytes in BOTH the worktree and the committed blob, bibliography fb0cf87e…, sidecar 7207fad1…, repro logs 416d4d48… and 9e21ad6a…. Rule-fixed-before-assessment ordering holds on independent evidence: HEAD 2ba291f committed 12:47:37 local, ka-s4-subset-rule.md mtime 12:53:00 (declared fixed_at 17:51:21Z = 12:51:21 local), completion-screen output 13:51:49, extraction 14:53:49 — monotone, and the rule's digest is embedded in the output it governs. All 197 rows carry `assessed_by: reading … the keyword classifier was not invoked`, `subset_rule: SR-1`, zero duplicates; verdicts 178/12/7 match the header; every include sits at `abstract` depth (the two sub-abstract depths land on `undecided`, not on includes); X6 is cited by no row, consistent with the stated reasoning. Thread B: the mutation test reproduces — flipping eru-0131 Q6 to `no` in se-extraction-primary.jsonl fails four assertions with `derived-only [('eru-0131','D5')]`, so the domain column is genuinely re-derived from the JSONLs and cannot pass off a hardcoded table; file restored and the suite is green (9 passed). Both documented entrypoints run: `uv sync --frozen --dry-run` exit 0, `uv run --frozen pytest` 9 passed, `uv run pre-commit --version` 4.6.2. Allocation (f) re-derived exactly from the arms field: 138 `arms == ['forward-citation']` / 59 carrying a topical arm, and 8,466 + 347 = 8,813. Correction (b) landed: arm4 attempted 88/88 with 68+19+1 = 88 outcomes and 41+88 = 129 records. Thread C: per-item retrieval route, byte count and SHA-256 are recorded for every load-bearing check, the four wrong-paper HTTP-200 application/pdf discards are named with the paper each actually was, the five prior digests reproduce for a fourth time, the boundary is stated ("58 of the 72 have never been read"), and the PWY pp.222-225 -> pp.222-224 and crash-monitor corrections are present in the review text (en-dash forms). Thread A extraction: 33 fulltext rows all carry an identity_check formed BEFORE reading, four flagged `verified-with-manifestation-caveat` with the manifestation named and page locators withheld, and the header states in terms that a 200 with content-type application/pdf is not evidence of identity. D6 verified on its concrete example: the kalshi review path has exactly one content state in `git rev-list --all`, hashing 4ee8ef348fbd5c19…, and the pinned acfc6a57a4ecdaa5… exists nowhere in history. NOT verified by me, and left as residual: the memo's full evidence_type distribution and the 140-retained / zero-empty-refutation counts (my generic walker did not match the trail schema and I did not hand-count 157 rows — the epistemics branch is better placed); the substantive Glosten-Milgrom / Kyle inventory-objective reversal (a literature-content judgment outside my scope, though I confirm the two rows it rests on are identity-checked, Kyle read as rendered page images and GM's support condition re-read as an image of p.76 rather than OCR-reconstructed); and whether 178 includes is substantively too permissive. Structural residual: the S4 artifacts whose digests the tracked prose now cites are still untracked, so until the delivery commit lands those exact bytes the deliverable pins working-tree state — the precise D6 failure mode, one commit away from recurring.

### literature-check

Citation hygiene in this session is unusually strong and I found no fabricated, non-resolving or wrongly-attributed source. 32 citations and method attributions were resolved to primary sources and checked. Everything I could reach independently matched, including the two items the focus brief flags as most consequential: (d) the Glosten-Milgrom / Kyle reversal is SUBSTANTIVELY CORRECT — GM's zero-expected-profit-per-trade condition and its inventory footnote verify, Kyle's price = E[v | order flow] sentence verifies verbatim at p.1316, and neither model carries an inventory objective, so Branch 2's H0 was indeed vacuous for both and the substitution to Ho & Stoll / Avellaneda & Stoikov / Guéant et al. is warranted (the only defect is the attribution FORM for Kyle, LITERATURE-1-1); and (h) the Thread C corrections I sampled hold against primary sources — LB-45's 0.048-0.392 reproduces Boswijk/Yu/Zu Table 1 cell-for-cell, and the discarded 0.048-0.282 is demonstrably a truncation at the c=0.3 column. Verified DOIs and metadata: Glosten & Milgrom 1985, Kyle 1985, Ho & Stoll 1981, Avellaneda & Stoikov 2008, Guéant/Lehalle/Fernandez-Tapia, Hanson 2007, Chen & Pennock 2007, Abernethy/Chen/Vaughan 2013, Li & Vaughan 2013, Chen & Vaughan 2010, Othman/Pennock/Reeves/Sandholm 2013 (author ORDER matches Crossref exactly), Kroer et al. 2016 (EC'16 161-178), Dudík/Wang/Pennock et al. 2021 (10.65109/bbzi6501 resolves, AAMAS 465-473), Krishnan 1992 (title is literally "An equivalence between the Kyle (1985) and the Glosten-Milgrom (1985) models" — the review's characterisation is exact), Chakraborty & Kearns 2011, Glosten & Harris 1988, Bartlett & O'Hara 2026, Bürgi et al. 2025, Gupta 2026, Feil & Nendel 2026, Moallemi et al. 2026, Feys 2026, Boswijk/Yu/Zu 2024. The Tanaka 2005 AG-10 claim is corroborated: 10.11179/ker.74.119 returns 404 from BOTH api.crossref.org and api.datacite.org. The three frozen-protocol passages quoted in ka-s4-subset-rule.md (§1.3 S4 row, §2.2 C3 and C4 clauses, §2.2 transfer clause) are verbatim against protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:152, :193-199, :201-208, and the C3→S4 mapping the composition argument relies on is in the criterion text itself ("see the transfer clause below (S4)"), so the subset rule is derived rather than reverse-engineered on the evidence available to my branch. All four memo citations verify and are characterised accurately: arXiv 2604.19049 does report 79% retrospective / 83% prospective kill rates in LLM-assisted defect discovery (and the memo's refusal to treat it as a benchmark is correct — it is a C/C++ security-library population); 2606.19544 is "Reliability without Validity", exactly the consistency-mistaken-for-validity failure mode the memo invokes; 2509.05396 documents debate sycophancy; 2602.09341 is about preserving minority branches over majority collapse. Residual: (1) quote-level re-checkability is asymmetric — every claim resting on a publisher or scanned PDF is attested by a digest of bytes that are not retained, and PDF rendering was unavailable to me, so roughly a third of the quoted strings could not be re-executed (LITERATURE-1-7); (2) I did not verify the 178 screen-abstract rows individually, the Hanson 2003 and 2011/2013 EC-series quotations, the Othman et al. restriction quotes, or the LB-25 crash-monitor passage against their sources; (3) records at [abs] and [meta] depth are cited for content in §8.4.2-8.4.3 (Tarnaud 2018, Peters/So/Ye 2007, Slamka et al. 2013 and the three bookmaker records) — each is marked, and the marked claims are title- or abstract-restatements rather than applicability conditions, which is the standard the spec set; (4) out of my scope and unchecked: the flow arithmetic and the 138/59 stratum allocation, the ER-RoB test's re-derivation, D6's git-history claim, and whether AG-11 should have been repaired rather than reported.

### format-auditor

Magic-numbers policy is in good shape on the artifacts I can check mechanically: the memo names every stipulated quantity as CONVENTION-1 through CONVENTION-5 with its status (conventional, post hoc, or stipulated-for-MDE) stated, the subset rule contains no numeric element at all and says so explicitly, and tests/test_erob_recount.py enforces the policy on itself via an AST scan with an allow-listed set of structural indices — that test is the strongest magic-number control in the repository and it cannot pass on a hardcoded published figure. Identity hygiene is clean: no real email address appears in any tracked artifact (the @gmail hits are third-party author affiliations inside retrieved OpenAlex/PubMed payloads, not the author's), the real name is present only where castles CLAUDE.md and ADR-0001 explicitly authorise it, and publishing.md is correctly out of force on this path. No unsubstituted <<KEY>> header placeholder or <<TODO>> body marker exists anywhere in docs/. Citation formatting for the load-bearing model set (Glosten & Milgrom 1985, Kyle 1985, Ho & Stoll 1981, Avellaneda & Stoikov 2008, Guéant/Lehalle/Fernandez-Tapia) is consistent in DOI, year and author casing across the protocol, review, agenda and spec. What I cannot cover from within this branch: whether the substantive Glosten-Milgrom/Kyle inventory-objective reversal is faithful to the quoted passages (literature-check), whether the 138/59 stratum allocation and the AG-11 identity failure are correctly handled (quant-auditor), whether the ER-RoB test genuinely re-derives rather than restates under adversarial mutation (code-reviewer), and whether the four wrong-paper HTTP 200 retrievals are all caught by the recorded identity checks (reproducibility-verifier). My two major findings are both numeric-consistency defects inside a memo whose subject is the auditability of the audit record, which is where such a defect is least affordable.

**The residual risk that dominates this round.** Two retained majors — QUANT-1-1 and QUANT-1-2 — turn on the same question the gate's one dropped critical (REV-1-1) also turned on: whether the 178 include verdicts are warranted under the frozen transfer clause. REV-1-1 was dropped on concrete counter-evidence, frozen rule J4, which composes the clause with X6 and forecloses an independent I1 judgement; that drop is sound and is not reopened here. What J4 does **not** supply, and what QUANT-1-1 and QUANT-1-2 correctly retain, is a per-record trace of the statement that decided each admission and a bound on how many admissions rest on only one conjunct. Until that is recorded, `n_included = 327` and every count derived from it are an upper bound whose lower bound is unreported. That is the single largest open exposure in the deliverable.

## ai-assistance-statement

Per [ICMJE Recommendations §V.A (updated January 2026)](https://www.icmje.org/recommendations/): AI cannot be an author; AI-assistance use is disclosed.

- **Models used:** Claude Opus 5 (model id `claude-opus-5`, Anthropic, via Claude Code / the Claude Agent SDK) — all branches: seven specialist audit agents (critical-reviewer at high effort, scope-auditor at medium, code-reviewer at medium, quant-auditor at high, reproducibility-verifier at medium, literature-check at high, format-auditor at low, each with `model: inherit` in its agent definition), the adversarial refuter (high effort) that gated all 22 critical/major dispositions, and the trail-assembly agent that authored this document.
- **Roles:** audit (findings, refutations, independent recomputation of the PRISMA flow and the A3 stratum allocation, an independent ER-RoB recount from the extraction JSONLs, an adversarial input mutation and suite re-run, live Crossref/DataCite/DOI-Handle resolution, re-fetching and digest-checking of source PDFs) and prose (assembly of this trail). No authored research content: the audited artifacts were not modified by this round's agents.
- **Sampling parameters** for these agents were provider defaults and are not recorded; the same gap is raised against the audited artifacts as REPRODUCIBILITY-1-3, and it applies to this audit as well. This round's dispositions are single-pass LLM outputs and a re-run is not guaranteed to reproduce them.
- **Note recorded rather than resolved:** REPRODUCIBILITY-1-1 (retained) concerns the audited 2026-09-03 stage's own missing ReproLog and sidecar. That is a property of the audited artifacts, not of this audit; the model id declared here is this audit's own.
- **Responsible human:** Sajan Koirala, who directed the audit-remediate loop and under whose review the verdict and the remediations proceed.
- **Reproducibility log:** ReproLog emission for this audit round is sequenced with the `/commit-with-provenance` step; the log lands at `logs/reproducibility/repro_log_{run_id}.json`, which is gitignored and therefore an **untracked locator**, and its clone-durable digest is carried by the commit's `Repro-Log-Path:` / `Repro-Log-SHA256:` trailers per CLAUDE.md §Reproducibility contract. The commit hash is to be recorded here as a dated addendum when it lands.

## immutability-and-retention

This trail and its JSON sidecar ([audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.json](audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.json), SHA-256 `6b3a8eae6921522a693380fbdc82b63df981417e86591dd41b5b208b11ee1ccf`, holding this round's payload verbatim per FAIR I1) are committed to the repository and are **append-only**, per 21 CFR 11.10(e): record changes must not obscure previously recorded information. Later rounds of this loop append new round sections to this file; corrections to any recorded entry are appended as a **dated addendum that identifies what it corrects** — prior entries are never edited, overwritten, or deleted. Retention follows the repository's git history; the tracked file is the durable record, and the front-matter digests bind it to the exact artifact states audited (HEAD `01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b`, `worktree_clean: false`). Six of the eleven audited artifacts were untracked at audit time, so their digests pin working-tree bytes until the delivery commit lands them — the exposure REPRODUCIBILITY-1-1 and the memo's D6 both name, recorded here rather than elided.

# Audit trail — S4 re-execution and repository reproducibility gaps, round 2

Round 2 of the three-round cap over the same four-thread deliverable. This round's task was to verify the twenty round-1 remediations and to hunt what they introduced. The same seven specialist branches ran in parallel over the remediated artifacts; every major finding then passed the adversarial refute gate before remediation was authorised. All seven branches returned `proceed-with-remediation`. The machine-readable payload for this round is held verbatim in the round-2 JSON sidecar named in the front matter above (FAIR I1); round 1's trail, table and sidecar are untouched.

Round-1 dispositions carried forward: Round 1 (2026-09-03): 7 branches routed (critical-reviewer, scope-auditor, code-reviewer, quant-auditor, reproducibility-verifier, literature-check, format-auditor), 31 agents. Counts: 1 raw critical, 21 raw major, 36 minor; 2 refuted; 20 to remediate. Refute gate DROPPED 2 with concrete counter-evidence: REV-1-1 (critical — claimed the 178 includes cite the transfer clause as their I1 warrant without the stage ever applying the clause's test) and SCOPE-1-1 (claimed Thread B item 1's decision half shipped but the tracking half did not). Do not re-raise these two unless new evidence defeats the refutation. The 20 retained: REV-1-2 (subset-rule priority self-attested), REV-1-3 and QUANT-1-4 (third and fourth false claim in amendment prose — four transfer statuses versus three), REV-1-4 (central S4 block self-contradiction), REV-1-5 (ADR-0005 header over-claim — LEAD FIXED), REV-1-6 and QUANT-1-6 (F-1 correction outside any governing record), CODE-1-1/1-2/1-3 (guard hole, hardcoded numerator, wrong annotation — SEPARATE AGENT FIXED), QUANT-1-1 (178 includes not separately evidenced), QUANT-1-2 (X6 reasoning one-sided), QUANT-1-3 (four records adjudicated from title alone), QUANT-1-5 (Thread C scoping rule self-attested), REPRODUCIBILITY-1-1 (no ReproLog for the largest run — LEAD FIXED), REPRODUCIBILITY-1-2 (VG register not updated), REPRODUCIBILITY-1-3 (LLM provenance pinned only to a model family), LITERATURE-1-1 (the Glosten-Milgrom/Kyle claim needs verification), FORMAT-1-1 and FORMAT-1-2 (two memo inconsistencies). 36 minors logged and NOT remediated; leave them logged unless one is demonstrably major.

## findings-table

One row per finding, carrying all eight schema fields (id, severity, category, location, issue, evidence, fix, reference) plus the raising branch and the disposition. Long cells are compressed here with `…`; the unabridged text of every field is in the round-2 JSON sidecar, and every gated claim is reproduced verbatim and unabridged in [refute-gate](#refute-gate) below. Totals: **0 critical, 20 major, 23 minor** raised; **4 refuted at the gate**; **16 routed to remediation**; **23 minors logged, not remediated**; **0 retained-conservative**.

## refute-gate

Every major finding was passed to an independent adversarial refuter before any remediation was authorised. A drop required concrete counter-evidence — a counter-test, a source quote, a reproduced check or a logical proof — never bare disagreement. Four of twenty were dropped; sixteen were retained. Each entry below carries the finding's claim VERBATIM and unabridged, the raiser, the refuter, the enumerated evidence type, and, where reproduction was required, the reproduction command and what it produced. No finding was retained-conservative this round (each retention rests on a failed refutation with stated evidence, not on an inconclusive gate).

### REV-2-2 — dropped

- **raised_by:** critical-reviewer
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `reproduced-check`
- **reproduction_required:** true
- **outcome:** dropped

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* interpretation
- *location:* docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:830 vs :2513-2516; docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2900-2917, :2918-2936, :3152-3156; docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:782-788

> **issue.** The four-versus-three correction (REV-1-3 / QUANT-1-4) landed at every site and the replacement counts are true — A18 §(b)'s table, the §8.4 caveat block and the agenda's rev-2 bullet all now read three changed, one confirmed, and §8.4.3 supports each of the four rows. But the corrected sentence re-asserts an attribution form that the same round established as wrong in LITERATURE-1-1. Frozen field E10 defines transfer status as the answer to "does the record ITSELF address bounded [0,1] payoffs with terminal settlement at an endpoint: addressed / not addressed / explicitly excluded". §8.4.3 assigns Kyle and Avellaneda & Stoikov `explicitly excluded` and Glosten & Milgrom `addressed`, and then states at each of those three entries, in terms, that the determination is the corpus's inference and not the authors' — marking it [corpus-inference]. A18 §(b)'s replacement sentence and the agenda bullet then summarise all four as resting "on a sentence the authors wrote", which is true of the assumption but not of the status. Three of the four headline yields of the extraction stage are therefore reported in an author-attributing vocabulary at the summary layer and disowned as corpus inferences at the record layer, with no statement anywhere of which reading of E10 the corpus adopted. This is exactly the defect LITERATURE-1-1 caught in the agenda's Kyle bullet, surviving one layer up.
>
> **evidence.** Protocol §5 field table: "Plus **transfer status** — does the record itself address bounded [0,1] payoffs with terminal settlement at an endpoint: `addressed` / `not addressed` / `explicitly excluded`". §8.4.3 Kyle: "Transfer status: **`explicitly excluded`, changed from `not addressed`.** … **[corpus-inference]** *… Kyle nowhere discusses binary or event contracts and nowhere says his model does or does not apply to them; the incompatibility is this corpus's inference from his stated assumption, and the corpus attributes no such statement to him.*" Against A18 §(b): "*\"Three E10 transfer statuses changed and one was confirmed at full text, and each rested on a sentence the authors wrote.\"*"
>
> **fix.** Add one sentence to §8.4.3's lead-in and to the §8.4 caveat block fixing the reading of E10 actually used — that `addressed` / `explicitly excluded` record whether the payoff support the record's own text STATES admits or forbids a [0,1] endpoint-settling payoff, and that the admission/exclusion step is this corpus's inference from that stated support in every case marked [corpus-inference], never an author's statement about binary contracts. Then restate the summary sentence at all three sites as "each rested on an applicability or support assumption the authors state; the transfer determination drawn from it is this corpus's inference and is marked as such per record." Record the reading as an append-only amendment, since it fixes a frozen field's vocabulary after use.
>
> **reference.** CHAMP items 27, 29 (causation/inference distinguished from what the source states); frozen protocol §5 field E10; audit finding LITERATURE-1-1 (2026-09-03)

**Refutation evidence.**

> The finding's load-bearing assertion — "with no statement anywhere of which reading of E10 the corpus adopted" — is false at all three sites carrying the disputed summary sentence. I reproduced the cited passages and then located, in each of the same three files, an explicit statement of exactly the reading the "claimed fix" asks to be added.
>
> 1) LIT REVIEW. The disputed caveat sentence is at docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:3151-3157 ("**Three transfer determinations changed and one was confirmed** on the strength of sentences the authors wrote"). Upstream in the SAME section 8.4, the "central S4 finding" block states the reading verbatim at :2316-2320: "The three process assumptions are ones a payoff confined to [0,1] and terminating at an endpoint **cannot** satisfy. The one payoff-support assumption is one it **can**. **[corpus-inference]** for the word 'cannot' and the word 'can' in that sentence — the authors state the assumptions; the compatibility judgement is this corpus's." Its four rows map one-to-one onto the four rows at issue: the one payoff-support statement is Glosten & Milgrom (`addressed`), the three process statements are Kyle, Avellaneda & Stoikov (the two changes to `explicitly excluded`) and Guéant et al. (the confirmation). Section 8.4's preamble additionally defines the marker generally at :2224-2227: "[corpus-inference] — a step this corpus takes from something an author states to something the author does not state."
>
> 2) PROTOCOL. A18 §(b)'s replacement sentence sits at :2513-2516, and it explicitly incorporates A17 §(e) ("The three changes and the confirmation are otherwise exactly as A17 §(e) records them"). A17 §(e) itself closes, at :2372-2376, with: "**The step from a stated assumption to an incompatibility is the corpus's inference, not the author's**, and every row that makes it flags it in a `corpus_inference_flag` field. Kyle does not say his model excludes binary contracts; he says the liquidation value is normal, and the corpus says a [0,1]-supported value is not a realisation of that. Those are different sentences and the file keeps them apart." That is the fix's requested sentence, already standing 140 lines above the sentence said to lack it, in the section the replacement sentence incorporates by reference.
>
> 3) AGENDA. The rev-2 bullet is at docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:782-793. The same file's Branch 2b states the reading per record at :448-450 ("**A payoff bounded in [0,1] realised at a terminal date meets that condition — and that inference is the corpus's, not the authors'.**") and :456-458 ("A value supported in [0,1] is not a realisation of a normal distribution — again the corpus's inference from the author's stated assumption, and not the author's statement.").
>
> Second, the claimed equivalence to LITERATURE-1-1 does not hold on the actual wording. LITERATURE-1-1's defect was an assertion that the authors STATED the proposition — the struck agenda text was "both say so in their own texts" (still recorded at :774-781, corrected to attribute the Kyle reading to the corpus). The corrected summary sentences attribute only the evidentiary BASIS: "rested on a sentence the authors wrote" (protocol :2513) / "on the strength of sentences the authors wrote" (lit review :3152) / "on the strength of sentences the authors wrote" (agenda :783). That predicate is true of all four rows and I verified each has a quoted author-stated assumption behind it in §8.4.3: G&M p. 76 "V ≥ 0, var(V) < ∞" (:2868-2870); Kyle p. 1317 normality (:2903-2906); Avellaneda & Stoikov §2.1 driftless arithmetic Brownian mid-price with mark-to-market at S_T (:2925-2933); Guéant et al. Brownian reference price with mark-to-market q_T S_T (:2938-2949). None of the four sentences says the authors made the transfer determination, so no author-attributing claim about the STATUS exists at the summary layer to contradict the record layer.
>
> The defect claim therefore fails on both of its components: the vocabulary is basis-attributing rather than status-attributing, and the reading of E10 the corpus adopted is stated explicitly in each of the three artifacts named.

**reproduction.**

```text
command:  grep -n 'corpus-inference' docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md; sed -n '2316,2320p;2224,2227p' docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md; sed -n '2372,2376p' docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md; sed -n '448,458p' docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md
observed: lit review :2316-2320 — "The three process assumptions are ones a payoff confined to [0,1] and terminating at an endpoint **cannot** satisfy. The one payoff-support assumption is one it **can**. **[corpus-inference]** for the word 'cannot' and the word 'can' in that sentence — the authors state the assumptions; the compatibility judgement is this corpus's."; lit review :2224-2227 defines the marker generally; protocol :2372-2376 — "**The step from a stated assumption to an incompatibility is the corpus's inference, not the author's**, and every row that makes it flags it in a `corpus_inference_flag` field."; agenda :448-450 and :456-458 state the same reading per record. The reading of E10 the finding says is stated nowhere is stated in all three files.
```

### REPRODUCIBILITY-2-2 — dropped

- **raised_by:** reproducibility-verifier
