**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* data_hashed
- *location:* docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:11; logs/reproducibility/repro_log_b47b1cf8777d494cadc68f5f90847bda.json (config_resolved_sha256); artifacts/runs/kalshi-arbitrage/b47b1cf8.../sidecar.json:48; ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl _header.subset_rule_sha256; ka-s4-subset-membership.json; ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl _header.prior_stage_files.subset_rule_sha256

> **issue.** The REV-1-2 remediation appended the self-attestation disclosure to ka-s4-subset-rule.md, converting 5f64d29b... from a whole-file digest into a prefix-only digest at every one of six external citation sites. Not one of those sites records the 10,189-byte bound; the byte count appears nowhere in the repository outside the appended section of the rule file itself. A verifier who hashes the named path gets df009a0d... and has no way to discover that a prefix was meant. The project's own convention for prefix digests exists and was not applied: the very same _header carries `protocol_frozen_prefix_bytes: 82677` beside `protocol_frozen_prefix_sha256`, but `subset_rule_sha256` has no bytes sibling.
>
> **evidence.** Recomputed: sha256(bytes[:10189]) = 5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6 (prefix holds, as the lead reported); sha256(whole 14,285 bytes) = df009a0de61a4a3c60d5fea66d5bef0b0937b18a983d387a997fa416375557fe. `grep -rn '10189\|10,189'` across docs/, artifacts/ and logs/ returns only DOI substrings — zero hits on the byte count. Sharpest instance: docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:11 places 5f64d29b... in a bare `sha256:` field inside the `artifacts:` block alongside six digests I re-verified as whole-file exact (lit review b8e63540..., extraction b7970a22..., agenda d9e7ca2a..., memo c3026d6f...). That row is now simply false as written, and audit_trail...md:501 states 'sha256 of ...ka-s4-subset-rule.md recomputes to 5f64d29b...' as a reproduced control check.
>
> **fix.** Add `subset_rule_prefix_bytes: 10189` (or `_prefix_sha256`/`_prefix_bytes` naming, mirroring `protocol_frozen_prefix_bytes`) beside every occurrence of `subset_rule_sha256` in the ReproLog, the sidecar, the completion-screen header, the membership file and the extraction header; publish the whole-file digest df009a0d... alongside; and correct the audit-trail `artifacts:` row so a prefix digest is not carried in a whole-file field.
>
> **reference.** none recorded

**Refutation evidence.**

> The finding's arithmetic reproduces but its three load-bearing evidentiary assertions are false; each is falsified by re-running the auditor's own cited command.
>
> WHAT REPRODUCES (not in dispute): sha256(bytes[:10189]) = 5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6; sha256(whole 14,285 bytes) = df009a0de61a4a3c60d5fea66d5bef0b0937b18a983d387a997fa416375557fe. Confirmed that ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl's _header carries protocol_frozen_prefix_bytes: 82677 beside protocol_frozen_prefix_sha256 while subset_rule_sha256 has no bytes sibling.
>
> FALSIFICATION 1 — the cited grep. The finding states: "grep -rn '10189\|10,189' across docs/, artifacts/ and logs/ returns only DOI substrings — zero hits on the byte count." I ran that exact pattern over those exact directories. It returns FIVE non-DOI hits on the byte count, four of them outside the rule file:
>   docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2058 — "file SHA-256 **`5f64d29b...`**, 10,189 bytes;"
>   docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2482
>   docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2487
>   docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2779
>   docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-rule.md:180
> The auditor's grep result as reported is not reproducible. Note protocol:2058 sits INSIDE amendment A16's own account of the rule — i.e. a citation site that does record the bound, adjacent to the digest.
>
> FALSIFICATION 2 — "the byte count appears nowhere in the repository outside the appended section of the rule file itself." False, per the four protocol hits above.
>
> FALSIFICATION 3 — the harm mechanism, "A verifier who hashes the named path gets df009a0d... and has no way to discover that a prefix was meant." False. protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2478-2489 is an explicit digest-supersession table under A18 that publishes BOTH digests with BOTH byte counts and enumerates the consuming sites verbatim:
>
>   | rule text **as applied by the screen** — the first **10,189 bytes** of the file | sha256 `5f64d29b6868670c4cafc15c89618405c6a64675e4dc62b3557235140ef2c6c6` |
>   | the file **as it now stands**, 14,285 bytes | sha256 `df009a0de61a4a3c60d5fea66d5bef0b0937b18a983d387a997fa416375557fe` |
>
> followed by: "Every artifact that cites `5f64d29b...` — the completion-screen header, the membership file, the extraction-file header, A16's *Artifacts* block, the ReproLog — **remains correct as a prefix digest over the first 10,189 bytes**, and those artifacts are deliberately **not** rewritten: they record the digest of the rule under which the screen actually ran, and rewriting them would falsify an execution record."
>
> That passage names five of the six sites the finding lists, states the bound, and states the deliberate non-rewrite rationale. A second independent discovery path exists at the named path itself: ka-s4-subset-rule.md:178-190 states "The first **10189 bytes** of this file... hash to `5f64d29b...`, which is the value cited by ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl's `_header`, by ka-s4-subset-membership.json, by ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl's `_header.prior_stage_files`, by amendment A16's *Artifacts* block and by the 2026-09-03 ReproLog's `config_resolved_sha256`."
>
> CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CLAIMED FIX: two of its three limbs are already satisfied in the repository — the bound is published beside the digest (protocol:2058, 2482, 2487, 2779) and the whole-file digest df009a0d... is published alongside it (protocol:2483). The finding was raised without checking the protocol, which is the governing document the amendments live in.
>
> RESIDUE, stated for completeness, insufficient to sustain the finding as framed: audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:11 does carry 5f64d29b... in a bare `sha256:` field, and :501 asserts a whole-file recomputation that no longer holds post-append (both were written before the REV-1-2 append and are stale, not misleading about a hidden prefix); and the five JSON/JSONL headers lack a `_prefix_bytes` sibling. That is a field-naming convention preference plus two stale audit-trail lines. The finding as written is a discoverability claim — "no way to discover that a prefix was meant", "appears nowhere in the repository" — and its entire major severity rests on undiscoverability. Undiscoverability is disproven by a published supersession table the auditor's own cited grep would have surfaced.

**reproduction.**

```text
command:  grep -rn '10189\|10,189' docs/ artifacts/ logs/
observed: FIVE non-DOI hits on the byte count, four of them outside the rule file: docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2058 ("file SHA-256 **`5f64d29b...`**, 10,189 bytes;"), :2482, :2487, :2779, and docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-subset-rule.md:180. The finding's reported result ("only DOI substrings — zero hits on the byte count") does not reproduce. protocol:2478-2489 additionally publishes both digests with both byte counts in an explicit supersession table.
```

### LITERATURE-2-2 — dropped

- **raised_by:** literature-check
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `source-quote`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** dropped

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* quotation not present in the cited source
- *location:* docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2968-2970; docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:394; docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl:26

> **issue.** The phrase *"genuinely outside the scope"* is presented as a verbatim quotation from Feys 2026, explicitly framed as author-stated scope ("Scope the author states against his own result"). The string does not occur in the paper. The nearest wordings are about different objects: the paper excludes path-functional preferences ("we exclude path-functional preferences such as intraday drawdown aversion"; "Adverse selection, queue position, intraday drawdown limits, and regime-switching risk aversion are outside the framework"), and separately uses "strictly outside the scope of the present paper" of Knightian ambiguity, not of path-functional preferences. This is the same defect class the session is remediating under LITERATURE-1-1 — corpus wording presented as author wording — and it survives inside the sentence the round-1 work left untouched.
>
> **evidence.** Two independent WebFetch passes over arXiv:2606.01477v3 (and one over v1). Pass 1 was asked to quote character-for-character every sentence containing "genuinely" and returned two, neither of which is about scope. Pass 2 was asked directly whether the exact string "genuinely outside the scope" appears and answered "No", returning the closest matches quoted above. The companion quotation in the same artifact sentence, "We do not model adverse selection", does verify verbatim, so the failure is specific to this string and not an artefact of the retrieval.
>
> **fix.** Strike the quotation marks and restate as the paper's own wording: Feys excludes path-functional preferences such as intraday drawdown aversion from the framework, and states that adverse selection, queue position, drawdown limits and regime-switching risk aversion are outside it. Correct the extraction-log row, which is the machine record that propagated the string.
>
> **reference.** https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01477 (Feys 2026), scope paragraph and §7.2

**Refutation evidence.**

> The string occurs in the source, verbatim, applied to exactly the object the artifact attributes it to. The finding's central factual claim ("The string does not occur in the paper") is false.
>
> METHOD. Rather than re-run WebFetch (which routes text through a summarizing model — the same channel that produced the finding), I retrieved the raw bytes and searched them myself: curl -sL https://arxiv.org/html/2606.01477v3 -> HTTP 200, 2,018,807 bytes. Identity confirmed from the fetched document itself: <title>Avellaneda-Stoikov and Cartea-Jaimungal as One Framework: A Forced Uniqueness Theorem for Inventory Market Making</title>, LaTeXML comment "Document created on May 30, 2026." This is v3, the same version the extraction log records. I stripped script/style/tags, unescaped entities, normalized curly quotes and whitespace to a 297,206-char plain-text file, then counted substrings.
>
> COUNTER-EVIDENCE (exact counts on that plain text):
>   "genuinely outside the scope"  -> 1
>   "genuinely outside"            -> 2
>   "outside the scope"            -> 2
>
> The occurrence at offset 61538, in the discussion following Proposition 7, reads character-for-character:
>
>   "Path-functional preferences in the strict sense (e.g., maximum-drawdown aversion or time-average wealth) are genuinely outside the scope of Proposition 7. We discuss this in Section 7, noting in particular that drawdown-averse market makers are not covered."
>
> That sentence is the author's own paper-voice prose, it contains the disputed string verbatim, and its subject is path-functional preferences exemplified by maximum-drawdown aversion — precisely the object the artifact attributes it to. Both halves of the finding therefore fail: the string is present, and it is not attached to the wrong referent.
>
> The artifact's rendering is accurate. lit_review:2969-2970 reads "path-functional preferences such as maximum-drawdown aversion are *"genuinely outside the scope"*"; research_agenda:394 reads "path-functional preferences are *"genuinely outside the scope"*"; the extraction-log row 26 reads "path-functional preferences in the strict sense, such as maximum-drawdown aversion or time-average wealth, are 'genuinely outside the scope'". All three track the source sentence. This is not the LITERATURE-1-1 defect class (corpus wording presented as author wording) — it is author wording presented as author wording.
>
> WHERE THE AUDITOR WENT WRONG. The finding correctly observes that "strictly outside the scope of the present paper" is used of Knightian ambiguity, and infers the disputed phrase must be a corruption of it. But these are two independent occurrences. Offset 49136 (Remark, Knightian-Ambiguity Qualification) reads "We treat it as strictly outside the scope of the present paper"; offset 61538 is the separate Proposition 7 sentence quoted above. Both "outside the scope" hits are accounted for and only one is about ambiguity.
>
> The finding's own supporting quotes are additionally unreliable. Of the two wordings it offers as the "nearest" actual text, one does not exist in the paper at all: "we exclude path-functional preferences" -> 0 occurrences. What the paper does contain, at §7.2 offset 197446, is "are genuinely outside this framework and cannot be absorbed into W^L_T by any reparametrization" — the second "genuinely outside", again about path-functional/drawdown preferences, reinforcing rather than undermining the artifact. So the retrieval channel that reported "No" to the existence question also fabricated a paraphrase and presented it in quotation marks.
>
> The companion quote verifies as the finding concedes: "We do not model adverse selection" -> 1 occurrence, in the scope paragraph ("Before proceeding, we note the scope of the present contribution. We do not model adverse selection, queue position, toxic flow, or the latency-sensitive components of execution"), which also confirms the artifact's framing of the sentence as author-stated scope.
>
> Local reproduction of the three cited locations succeeded (docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2968-2970; docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:394; docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl:26) — the text is as quoted. The defect claim about that text is what fails. No remediation is warranted; applying the claimed fix would replace a correct verbatim quotation with a paraphrase and would corrupt an accurate machine extraction record.
>
> Residual note, not the claimed defect and not grounds for the finding: the prose sites truncate "of Proposition 7", leaving "the scope" unqualified. §7.2's "genuinely outside this framework" makes the unqualified reading non-misleading, so this is at most cosmetic.

### FORMAT-2-3 — dropped

- **raised_by:** format-auditor
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `counter-test`
- **reproduction_required:** true
- **outcome:** dropped

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* magic-numbers
- *location:* docs/literature/search_logs/explosive-regime/se-verify-loadbearing-01.json:41 (`counts.sec7_candidate_denominator.quoted_span_regex`)

> **issue.** The quoted-span scan that produces `quoted_span_occurrences: 30` — which feeds `candidate_occurrences_total: 107`, the denominator the log adds specifically to answer round-1 finding QUANT-1-5 — is bounded by two bare numeric thresholds, a 3-character minimum and a 500-character maximum, with no justification anywhere in the file. The log documents the scan's scope, its non-semantic character and its unit mismatch, but not why the span bounds are 3 and 500. Both choices move the reported denominator: raising the floor drops short quoted fragments, lowering the ceiling drops long block quotations. A remediation added to supply a missing denominator therefore supplies one whose two free parameters are undeclared.
>
> **evidence.** "quoted_span_regex": "\"([^\"\\n]{3,500})\" - section 7 carries no curly quotation marks"; and the adjacent "reading": "Against a section-7 candidate pool of 107 occurrences (54 distinct strings), the load-bearing set took 45."
>
> **fix.** Add a sibling key `quoted_span_bounds_rationale` stating what 3 and 500 exclude and reporting the denominator's sensitivity — e.g. the occurrence count under {1,inf} and under the chosen bounds — so a reader can see whether 107 is robust to them. If the bounds are pure conveniences, say so and label them CONVENTION as the memo and the protocol do elsewhere.
>
> **reference.** ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md §"Parameter & Prompt Selection" — zero arbitrary thresholds; memo §8 CONVENTION-naming precedent

**Refutation evidence.**

> I ran the exact sensitivity test the finding itself names as the missing evidence ("the occurrence count under {1,inf} and under the chosen bounds — so a reader can see whether 107 is robust to them"). The answer is that 107 is exactly, provably robust: the bounds are strictly non-binding and neither one moves the reported denominator.
>
> WHAT REPRODUCES (not disputed): line 41 of C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\search_logs\explosive-regime\se-verify-loadbearing-01.json reads verbatim "quoted_span_regex": "\"([^\"\n]{3,500})\" - section 7 carries no curly quotation marks", and a grep of the whole file for "500", "bounds", "rationale" and "CONVENTION" returns no rationale key for 3 or 500 (only line 52's unrelated use of the word "bounds" and line 1313's CONVENTION label on r0). The bare numerals are there, undocumented.
>
> WHAT IS DISPROVED (the finding's load-bearing step): the assertion "Both choices move the reported denominator: raising the floor drops short quoted fragments, lowering the ceiling drops long block quotations", and its conclusion that the remediation "supplies one whose two free parameters are undeclared".
>
> Counter-test. I reconstructed the scan on both the version the log pins and the current file, applying the log's two regexes verbatim:
>
>   HEAD 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b: bounded{3,500}=22 quoted spans; unbounded{1,inf}=22. IDENTICAL. Span lengths min=6 max=53.
>   Working tree (section 7 = lines 1485-2059): bounded{3,500}=30 quoted spans (28 distinct), locators 77 (26 distinct), candidate_occurrences_total=107 — reproducing the log's counts 77/26/30/28/107 exactly; unbounded{1,inf}=30 (28 distinct), total 107. IDENTICAL.
>
> So candidate_occurrences_total = 107 under {3,500} and 107 under no bounds at all. The reported denominator is invariant to the deletion of both parameters.
>
> Why it is invariant, not a coincidence of the chosen values: across the ENTIRE review file (not just section 7) there are 265 double-quoted spans; the shortest is 3 characters and the longest is 276. Zero spans fall below 3 and zero exceed 500 anywhere in the document. The floor of 3 excludes nothing that exists; the ceiling of 500 is more than four times the longest span in section 7 (112) and nearly double the longest in the whole file. Both bounds sit outside the support of the data they filter. The admissible-invariance region is wide on both sides: any floor <= 6 and any ceiling >= 112 returns exactly 107.
>
> The finding's stated mechanism therefore does not obtain. 3 and 500 are not free parameters of the reported number — they are regex-engine catastrophic-backtracking guards whose values are provably inert on this input, and 107 carries no undeclared tuning at all. The concern CLAUDE.md §"Parameter & Prompt Selection" governs is a tunable value that drives a reported result; a bound that returns the identical result when removed entirely is not driving anything. The residual — that the file does not spell out why 3 and 500 — is true but is a documentation nicety about an inert constant, not the defect claimed, which was that the QUANT-1-5 remediation introduced a parameter-dependent denominator.
>
> Reproduce with: git show 01ecfe78:docs/literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md, slice section 7, and compare re.findall('"([^"\n]{3,500})"', seg) against re.findall('"([^"\n]+)"', seg).

**reproduction.**

```text
command:  git show 01ecfe78:docs/literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md, slice section 7, then compare re.findall('"([^"\\n]{3,500})"', seg) against re.findall('"([^"\\n]+)"', seg); repeat on the working-tree file (section 7 = lines 1485-2059)
observed: HEAD 01ecfe78: bounded{3,500}=22 quoted spans, unbounded{1,inf}=22 — IDENTICAL (span lengths min 6, max 53). Working tree: bounded=30 (28 distinct), locators 77 (26 distinct), candidate_occurrences_total=107; unbounded=30 (28 distinct), total 107 — IDENTICAL. Across the whole review file: 265 double-quoted spans, shortest 3 chars, longest 276; zero fall below 3 and zero exceed 500. Any floor <= 6 and any ceiling >= 112 returns exactly 107, so the two bounds are provably inert on this input.
```

### REV-2-1 — retained

- **raised_by:** critical-reviewer
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* consistency
- *location:* docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:43, :117-123, :126, :787, :891, :3444-3448, :3928 vs :3105-3112

> **issue.** ADJUDICATION OF THE CENTRAL CLAIM. I verified every mechanical count in the §8.4.4 / A18 §(d) block against ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl and all of them hold: 197 verdict rows; 178 include / 12 exclude / 7 undecided; `criterion_cited` takes exactly two distinct values across the 178 includes with byte-identical boilerplate on 177 and one bespoke value (line 50, U01611); zero rows carry primary_code X6 (the 13 X6 mentions are all in rationale prose on the 12 X5 rows and one undecided row); and the file contains zero escaped quotation marks, so 0 of 178 rows quote the record's own text. The adjudication AGAINST the session's own interest is therefore correct and well evidenced, and declaring the range [0,178] while keeping the 178 in the corpus IS defensible in principle — the 178 carry no §8 claim line, and their only effect on §8.4 is to weaken universal negatives, which is the conservative direction. The defect is PLACEMENT, not logic. A18 §(d) issues a binding instruction — "no consumer of this protocol may read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits" — and the corpus record then does exactly that at every accounting site. `n_included: 327` is published as a definite value in the frontmatter, in the revision note, in the §5 identity table (reported as holding, "yes"), in the flow narrative, in AG-11, in §13.2 and in the agenda's own frontmatter, with no [149,327] qualifier at any of them; §8.8 converts it into a headline rate, "59% of the included corpus supports no claim in the synthesis (194 of 327)", as a point value on a denominator the artifact's own governing amendment says is unestablished. The qualifier appears at exactly one body site (§8.4.4), one clause of §13.1, one traceability-table cell, and buried inside the frontmatter `protocol_amendments` string. Verdict on the lead's question: Thread A shipped a corpus, not an open defect with a corpus attached — but it shipped it with the headline count asserted unconditionally at every site a reader actually reads it from, which is the one thing A18 forbids.
>
> **evidence.** A18 §(d): "**Until it is performed, no consumer of this protocol may read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits.**" Against frontmatter: "n_included: 327   # WAS 149. 149 + the 178 amendment A16 admitted." and §8.8: "**The honest headline for this subsection is therefore: 59% of the included corpus supports no claim in the synthesis** (194 of 327), against 11% (16 of 149) before the re-execution." and "Read this first" item 5: "They were included on a stage-2 eligibility reading of their own text, which is a stronger warrant than any of the 8,466 classifier dispositions carry" — with no upper-bound qualifier at any of the three.
>
> **fix.** Propagate the bound to every site that publishes a count derived from the 178, without changing any count: (a) add to the frontmatter `n_included:` comment the clause "178 of these rest on the negation-of-X6 admission bar; the set the transfer clause admits lies in [0,178], so the include count is bounded [149,327] — see §8.4.4 and A18 §(c)-(d)"; (b) add the same clause as a sixth numbered item, or an appendix to item 5, of "Read this first"; (c) footnote the §5 identity table's 327 row and the AG-11 row; (d) restate §8.8's 59% as "194 of 327 on the include count as recorded; the ratio inherits the [0,178] bound and is not a point estimate"; (e) mirror in the agenda frontmatter's `corpus:` comment. No re-screen is required and no count moves.
>
> **reference.** CHAMP items 19, 22, 27; SAMPL §Reporting numbers (a rate must carry the denominator it is conditional on); protocol amendment A18 §(c)-(d)

**Refutation evidence.**

> Attempted refutation failed; every element of the finding reproduces at the stated locations.
>
> (1) A18 §(d) exists verbatim in [docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md](docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md) (~line 2618): "Until it is performed, no consumer of this protocol may read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits." The same block (lines 2605-2606) states "The set the transfer clause admits lies somewhere in [0, 178] ... 178 is the upper bound. The lower bound recoverable from the artifact as written is 0."
>
> (2) Grep for every bound-carrying string ([0,178] / [0, 178] / [149,327] / "upper bound") across the lit review, the agenda, the subset-rule log and the protocol returns exactly the four sites the finding names inside the lit review: line 3105 (§8.4.4 body), line 3850 (§13.1 A18 clause), line 4445 (§13.9 traceability cell for QUANT-1-2), and the frontmatter `protocol_amendments` string (line 29). No other occurrence exists anywhere in [docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md](docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md) or in [docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md](docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md).
>
> (3) Every accounting site the finding lists publishes 327 unconditionally, confirmed by reading each: frontmatter line 43 `n_included: 327   # WAS 149. 149 + the 178 amendment A16 admitted.`; "Read this first" item 5 (lines 117-123), which asserts the 178 rest on "a stage-2 eligibility reading of their own text, which is a stronger warrant than any of the 8,466 classifier dispositions carry" with no bound; the §5 identity table line 787 `| n_screened - n_excluded == n_included | 8,813 - 8,486 = 327 | 327 | yes |`; the flow narrative line 891 "**327** records are included."; AG-11 at line 3988 and its §5 restatement at 788-800; §13.2 line 3928; and the agenda frontmatter line 28 `corpus: ... # 327 records after amendment A16`. §8.8 at lines 3444-3446 reads "**The honest headline for this subsection is therefore: 59% of the included corpus supports no claim in the synthesis** (194 of 327), against 11% (16 of 149) before the re-execution" — a point-value rate on the unqualified denominator.
>
> (4) The one candidate counter-argument — that `n_included: 327` is a bookkeeping record of what was included rather than a claim about transfer-clause admission — is defeated by the protocol's own repair text immediately above A18 §(d): the repair requires "a restatement of the include count and of every downstream count that uses 327." The protocol itself treats 327 and its derived counts as contingent on the un-performed re-screen, which corroborates the finding rather than refuting it.
>
> No counter-test, source quote, or logical proof contradicts the placement defect. The claim stands.

### REV-2-3 — retained

- **raised_by:** critical-reviewer
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* consistency
- *location:* docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:73-75, :435-438

> **issue.** LITERATURE-1-1's remediation is incomplete by two sites. The [corpus-inference] marker was added to the Branch 2 Kyle bullet (line 294-311) and to the Branch 2 model table's struck Kyle row (line 341), and the rev-2 corrections bullet at line 773-781 carries it. Two further sites in the same file still state the claim as established fact with no marker: the branch-count paragraph in "How to read this document", and — materially — Branch 2b's "Why this branch exists" paragraph, which is the entire warrant for creating a new branch. Since the Kyle half of that claim is an inference from absence over pp.1315-1317 read as page images, with the remaining pages unread, the site that founds a new research branch is the one site where the marker is load-bearing and it is the one site that lacks it.
>
> **evidence.** Line 73-75: "**Branch 2b is new at rev 2**: the full texts showed that two records rev 1 had filed under Branch 2 are adverse-selection models with no inventory objective, so their transfer question is a different question and now has its own branch rather than being lost." Line 436-437: "Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle are *adverse-selection* models with zero-expected-profit makers and no inventory state."
>
> **fix.** Add the same qualification the Branch 2 bullet carries to both sites: "— Glosten & Milgrom by their own footnote on binding inventory constraints; Kyle by this corpus's reading of the specification over pp. 1315-1317, the only pages read, which is an inference from absence **[corpus-inference]**". Branch 2b's precondition list should additionally record that its own founding premise for Kyle is unverified over the unread remainder of the article.
>

### CODE-2-1 — retained

- **raised_by:** code-reviewer
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* errors
- *location:* C:\Users\skoir\castles\tests\test_erob_recount.py:514-531

> **issue.** `divergent_cells` fails OPEN on an unrecognised domain-concern label. A recorded D cell is counted as a lenient divergence only if it equals `low`, and as a strict divergence only if it is in {`unclear`,`high`}; any other text (case variant `Low`, a typo, a level a successor instrument adds, an unstripped glyph) falls through both branches and is silently treated as non-divergent. No test constrains the D-column vocabulary, unlike the Q column, which has `test_every_answer_parses_to_a_declared_scale_level`. An unmarked cell carrying such a value would be dropped from the census and BOTH `test_divergence_profile_matches_amendment_a8` and `test_no_divergent_cell_outside_the_marked_set` would still pass, so the A8 reconciliation would be green over an incomplete census. This is inconsistent with the module's own declared posture elsewhere (`_leading_answer` asserts loudly; the guard's `_FREE_DIGITS` comment says an unfamiliar construct 'fails closed and is corrected in the open'). Verified latent, not active: no D cell in the shipped section 6 table currently carries a non-conforming label.
>
> **evidence.** recorded = strip_markers(row[domain.lower()])\n            if recorded in NOT_ASSESSABLE_CELL:\n                continue\n            if rule[index] == RULE_RAISED and recorded == LOW_CONCERN_LABEL:\n                lenient.append(...)\n            elif rule[index] == RULE_LOW and recorded in RAISED_CONCERN_LABELS:\n                strict.append(...)
>
> **fix.** After the `NOT_ASSESSABLE_CELL` guard, assert membership in the legend's declared vocabulary before classifying: `assert recorded in RAISED_CONCERN_LABELS | {LOW_CONCERN_LABEL}, f"{row['rec']} {domain}: domain-concern cell outside protocol section 6's low/high/unclear scale: {recorded!r}"`. Protocol section 6 line 521 declares the scale as `low / high / unclear`, so the set is document-derived, not a new constant.
>
> **reference.** protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md line 521 ("domain-level concern judgments (low / high / unclear)")

**Refutation evidence.**

> Refutation attempted and failed; every check corroborated the finding.
>
> 1. Code reproduced verbatim at C:\Users\skoir\castles\tests\test_erob_recount.py:514-531. The two branches are `if rule[index] == RULE_RAISED and recorded == LOW_CONCERN_LABEL` / `elif rule[index] == RULE_LOW and recorded in RAISED_CONCERN_LABELS`, with no else and no vocabulary assertion after the `NOT_ASSESSABLE_CELL` continue. Constants at lines 128-130: NOT_ASSESSABLE_CELL = {EM_DASH, "n/a", ""}, RAISED_CONCERN_LABELS = {"unclear","high"}, LOW_CONCERN_LABEL = "low".
>
> 2. Counter-test executed (scratchpad ct.py, loading the test module and calling divergent_cells on a synthetic one-row table where D1's rule derives to R). Output:
>    rule: RLLLLLLL
>    'low'   -> {'lenient': [('R1','D1')], 'strict': []}
>    'Low'   -> {'lenient': [], 'strict': []}
>    'lowe'  -> {'lenient': [], 'strict': []}
>    'some'  -> {'lenient': [], 'strict': []}
>    'LOW '  -> {'lenient': [], 'strict': []}
>    A genuine lenient divergence is silently dropped, with no exception and no failure, for any label outside the exact string "low". Fail-open confirmed.
>
> 3. No other consumer constrains the D-column vocabulary. `row[domain.lower()]` appears only twice in the module: line 524 (this classification) and line 751, which tests only for the presence of the SECTION_SIGN/PILCROW glyphs in the raw cell and never inspects the label. test_rule_derived_domain_column_reproduces_the_shipped_one reads the separate `d_rule` column, not the D cells. test_every_answer_parses_to_a_declared_scale_level (line 624) asserts `levels <= {"yes","no","unclear","partial"}` for the Q column only, confirming the asymmetry the finding names.
>
> 4. Cited source verified: docs/methodology/protocol_explosive-regime-review_2026-08-24.md contains "domain-level concern judgments (low / high / unclear)" in the ER-RoB v1 instrument declaration, so the vocabulary is document-derived as claimed.
>
> 5. The "both tests still pass" claim holds for the stated scenario: a corrupted-label cell that is also unmarked is absent from `found` and absent from `marked_lenient`/`marked_strict`, so test_no_divergent_cell_outside_the_marked_set compares equal sets, and test_divergence_profile_matches_amendment_a8 sees counts that never included it. (A corrupted label on a cell that IS marked would fail the marked-set test — but that is not the case the finding asserts.)
>
> No counter-evidence obtained; the defect claim is true and latent as described.

### QUANT-2-1 — retained

- **raised_by:** quant-auditor
- **severity_claimed:** major

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* reproducibility
- *location:* docs/decisions/ADR-0005-lockfile-is-the-pinning-mechanism.md:37-49; docs/literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md:3598 (VG-14 row) and :4188 (§15 environment-specification row)

> **issue.** The uv.lock provenance pin does not resolve: the cited (commit, SHA-256) pair names two different file states, and the cited line count belongs to the third. All three sites assert `uv.lock` is "committed at 2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433, SHA-256 eca78f9d52a8534f2890c99bd2f16b3aadc1455b0eb7b93c6d2e621240efd885 ... 1,467 lines". At 2ba291f the blob is 208,360 bytes / 1,467 lines / sha256 d2a0b28183f1d74260cd56bcd2881febf9e97d6d2e9dc58d07200db61c478597. eca78f9d… is the file at HEAD 01ecfe7 (208,234 bytes / 1,473 lines). The two states differ materially, not cosmetically (`source = { editable = "." }` → `{ virtual = "." }`, and the dev dependency group is restructured under `[package.dev-dependencies]`), so the digest and the commit pin different dependency graphs. This defect was INTRODUCED by the round-1 REV-1-5 remediation: the ADR states in terms that the commit hash was added at that fix ("The first draft of this ADR asserted 'is committed' in the present tense while the file was still untracked, naming no commit — Corrected after audit finding REV-1-5"), and it reproduces exactly the failure the ADR itself names, "a digest whose referent is unreachable from a clone pins nothing". The repository already contains the correct digest for that state: the round-3 ReproLog row at review:4182 records `env_id = d2a0b28183f1d74260cd56bcd2881febf9e97d6d2e9dc58d07200db61c478597` for uv.lock.
>
> **evidence.** $ git show 2ba291f:uv.lock | sha256 → bytes 208360 lines 1467 sha256 d2a0b28183f1d74260cd56bcd2881febf9e97d6d2e9dc58d07200db61c478597
> $ git show 01ecfe7:uv.lock | sha256 → bytes 208234 lines 1473 sha256 eca78f9d52a8534f2890c99bd2f16b3aadc1455b0eb7b93c6d2e621240efd885
> $ git diff --stat 2ba291f 01ecfe7 -- uv.lock → 1 file changed, 20 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)
> ADR-0005:37-49 (quoted): "`uv.lock` is committed at **`2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433`**, SHA-256 **`eca78f9d…`** … It resolves the full dependency graph — 1,467 lines"
>
> **fix.** At all three sites, repoint the pin to the commit that actually carries the cited bytes: `uv.lock` at HEAD `01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b`, SHA-256 `eca78f9d52a8534f2890c99bd2f16b3aadc1455b0eb7b93c6d2e621240efd885`, 1,473 lines; and record that the file was first tracked at `2ba291f` in a materially different resolution whose digest is `d2a0b281…`, so the supersession is visible rather than absorbed. Do not simply swap the line count — the two-state history is the fact worth recording.
>
> **reference.** CLAUDE.md §Reproducibility contract; ADR-0005 §Context ("a digest whose referent is unreachable from a clone pins nothing"); verification script: git show <rev>:uv.lock | sha256sum

**Refutation evidence.**

> REFUTATION FAILED — every element of the finding reproduced independently; no counter-evidence found.
>
> 1. Digest counter-test (git show <rev>:uv.lock | sha256sum, run by me):
>    2ba291f -> sha256 d2a0b28183f1d74260cd56bcd2881febf9e97d6d2e9dc58d07200db61c478597, 1467 lines, 208360 bytes
>    01ecfe7 (HEAD) -> sha256 eca78f9d52a8534f2890c99bd2f16b3aadc1455b0eb7b93c6d2e621240efd885, 1473 lines, 208234 bytes
>    Both match the auditor's reported values exactly. The cited (commit 2ba291f, SHA-256 eca78f9d...) pair is therefore inconsistent: eca78f9d is not the blob at 2ba291f.
>
> 2. Text reproduced at all three cited sites, at the stated line numbers:
>    - ADR-0005:37-49 verbatim: "`uv.lock` is committed at **`2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433`**, SHA-256 **`eca78f9d52a8534f2890c99bd2f16b3aadc1455b0eb7b93c6d2e621240efd885`** ... It resolves the full dependency graph — 1,467 lines". Lines 41-45 confirm the auditor's provenance claim verbatim: "The first draft of this ADR asserted \"is committed\" in the present tense while the file was still untracked, naming no commit ... Corrected after audit finding REV-1-5."
>    - lit_review:3598 (VG-14 row) carries the same 2ba291f + eca78f9d + "1,467 lines" triple.
>    - lit_review:4188 (§15 environment-specification row) carries the same 2ba291f + eca78f9d pair.
>    - lit_review:4182 confirms the corroborating fact: round-3 ReproLog row records `env_id` = `d2a0b28183f1d74260cd56bcd2881febf9e97d6d2e9dc58d07200db61c478597` for uv.lock — i.e. the repository already holds the correct digest for the 2ba291f state.
>
> 3. Closed the only available defense (LF-checkout-form). The ADR justifies its digest as taken "over the LF checkout form that `.gitattributes` (`uv.lock text eol=lf`) makes identical on every platform". I verified .gitattributes:16 = `uv.lock text eol=lf`, and that BOTH blobs contain zero CR bytes (grep -c $'\r' -> 0 at each rev). Blob bytes therefore equal checkout bytes at both revisions, so no normalization convention can reconcile eca78f9d with 2ba291f. The working-tree file confirms the assignment independently: sha256sum uv.lock -> eca78f9d..., wc -l -> 1473, matching HEAD, not 2ba291f.
>
> 4. Materiality confirmed, not cosmetic: git diff --stat 2ba291f 01ecfe7 -- uv.lock -> 1 file changed, 20 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-); the diff shows `source = { editable = "." }` -> `{ virtual = "." }`, the dev group moved out of `[package.optional-dependencies]` into `[package.dev-dependencies]` with a new `[package.metadata.requires-dev]` table, and `provides-extras` reduced from ["dev","data","viz"] to ["data","viz"]. The two states describe different dependency graphs.
>
> Only imprecision found, which does not disturb the defect: the finding's phrase "the cited line count belongs to the third [state]" is loose — 1,467 lines is the line count of the 2ba291f state, i.e. of the cited commit, not of a third distinct state. That is a descriptive quibble about which of the two states the line count matches; the load-bearing claim (the cited commit and the cited SHA-256 name different file states, so the pin does not resolve) is fully confirmed at all three sites.

> **issue.** The candidate denominator added by the round-1 QUANT-1-5 remediation is pinned to a repository state that does not produce it, and it is measured on a §7 that this same pass's corrections had already enlarged. The log declares `scan_scope: "…lines 1467-2040 as of git HEAD 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b, i.e. the whole of section 7 up to the section-8 heading"` and reports locator_occurrences 77 / distinct 26, quoted_span_occurrences 30 / distinct 28, candidate_occurrences_total 107 / distinct 54. Running the log's own two declared regexes against §7 at that HEAD (§7 = lines 1467–2039 there) returns 69/22 and 22/20 = 91/42. The published figures reproduce exactly — 77/26/30/28/107/54 — only against the POST-remediation working-tree file, where §7 runs 1485–2059. Two consequences: (i) an auditor who checks out 01ecfe7 as instructed gets 91/42 and concludes the log is wrong; (ii) ~16 of the 107 occurrences are locators and quotations that this pass's own six corrections wrote into §7 (e.g. "Theorems 6–7 p.1088", "pp.222–224", "eq. (2.8)"), so they were never candidates for the verification and their inclusion inflates the stated residual ("~62 occurrences") and understates the coverage ratio the remediation exists to make auditable.
>
> **evidence.** At HEAD 01ecfe7 (§7 = 1467–2039): locator occ 69, distinct 22; quoted occ 22, distinct 20; total 91 / 42.
> At the working tree (§7 = 1485–2059): locator occ 77, distinct 26; quoted occ 30, distinct 28; total 107 / 54 — byte-for-byte the log's reported figures.
> Log text: "scan_scope: docs/literature/lit_review_explosive-regime-dating_2026-08-24.md lines 1467-2040 as of git HEAD 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b"; "reading: … Against a section-7 candidate pool of 107 occurrences (54 distinct strings), the load-bearing set took 45. The residual ~62 occurrences …"
>
> **fix.** Correct `scan_scope` to name the state actually scanned — the post-correction §7 (lines 1485–2059) at the delivery commit, cited by that commit's digest of the review file, not by 01ecfe7 — and add a two-line note giving the pre-correction figures (91 occurrences / 42 distinct at 01ecfe7 §7 = 1467–2039) so the reader can see that ~16 occurrences were authored by this pass and are not part of the candidate pool it drew from. State the coverage ratio against the pre-correction pool.
>
> **reference.** Deliverable spec Thread C check ("the count of records verified is stated against the 72-record corpus so the residual is visible"); memo_refute-gate-calibration_2026-09-03.md §2.3 D6, which names this exact failure mode (a digest pinned to an uncommitted working-tree state)

**Refutation evidence.**

> Attempted refutation failed — the finding reproduces exactly, in every particular.
>
> 1. Section boundaries. Scanning for `^#{1,3}\s*7[.\s]` / `^#{1,3}\s*8[.\s]`: at git HEAD 01ecfe7822ccca794272c35956ac8f8289d0c20b the §7 heading is line 1467 and the §8 heading is line 2039 (file 4418 lines). In the working tree the same headings are at 1485 and 2059 (file 4625 lines). The log's declared `scan_scope` "lines 1467-2040 ... as of git HEAD 01ecfe7" therefore names the HEAD state, not the working-tree state.
>
> 2. Counts at the declared HEAD. Running the log's own two declared regexes (locator_regex verbatim, quoted_span_regex as `"([^"\n]{3,500})"`) over HEAD §7: locator occurrences 69, distinct 22; quoted-span occurrences 22, distinct 20; total 91 occurrences / 42 distinct. Identical for either end bound (1467-2038 and 1467-2039 both give 69/22/22/20). This contradicts the log's published 77/26/30/28/107/54.
>
> 3. Counts at the working tree. Same regexes over working-tree §7 (1485-2059, and identically 1485-2058): locator 77 / distinct 26, quoted 30 / distinct 28, total 107 / distinct 54 — byte-for-byte the six published fields. So the figures are pinned to an uncommitted post-remediation state while the log cites a commit that does not produce them. The auditor-follows-instructions consequence (i) holds: checking out 01ecfe7 yields 91/42.
>
> 4. Contamination consequence (ii) also holds. Multiset diff of HEAD §7 vs working-tree §7 under the same regexes: locators added by this pass = p.1088, p.221, pp.1086–1087, pp.222–224, Table 1, Table 3, Table 4, Theorem 6, Theorem 7 (9 occurrences), locators removed = pp.222–225 (1); quoted spans added = 8 (including "As a compromise, we use gamma = 0.35 in the empirical study", "crash monitor never signals before the crash by construction", "different scaling", "different scaling, different sample size", "is not possible", "r2e and r2f are not consistent estimators...", "we consider here closed-ended procedures...", "we recommend gamma = .45 to achieve fast and reliable detection"). Net +16 occurrences (91 - 1 + 17 = 107), matching the finding's "~16 of the 107 occurrences ... this pass's own six corrections wrote into §7". `eq. (2.8)` likewise appears once in working-tree §7. Those 16 were authored by the remediation and were not candidates the verification drew from, so the stated residual "~62" and the 45-of-107 ratio are computed against an inflated pool (pre-correction pool is 91, giving 45-of-91 and a residual of ~46).
>
> Both the pinning defect and the pool-inflation defect are confirmed by direct re-execution. No counter-evidence found; the claim stands as stated.

### QUANT-2-3 — retained

- **raised_by:** quant-auditor
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* reporting
- *location:* docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:12-14 (front-matter revision_note), :274, :313-317; docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2738-2741 (A18 §(g))

> **issue.** The LITERATURE-1-1 marker is not complete. The corpus record's own rule is explicit — §8.4's legend defines [corpus-inference] as "a step this corpus takes from something an author states to something the author does not state. Every such step is flagged here and in the extraction file" — and the remediation correctly applies it at the two Branch-2 sites (agenda :294-311 and the model-set table row :341). But the machine-readable `revision_note`, which is the one field a tool reads, asserts flatly that "TWO of the five records it named as 'named-lineage anchors' … turn out not to be inventory-control models at all -- Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle", with no marker. The body establishes only the Glosten & Milgrom half from the authors' text (the zero-profit/binding-inventory footnote); the Kyle half is, in the body's own words, "an inference **from an absence** -- not a statement of Kyle's", established "over the three pages read and not over the article" (pp. 1315-1317, read as page images). The same unmarked assertion appears at agenda :274 ("**Neither model has an inventory objective.**") and in the load-bearing consequence paragraph :313-317 ("vacuous for Glosten & Milgrom and for Kyle, because there is no such result in either model to re-derive"), which is the stated ground for removing Kyle from the model set. A18 §(g) in the append-only protocol likewise records "The body establishes two — Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle" without the qualifier. The substantive claim is very likely correct; what is wrong is that a three-page inference-from-absence is published as an established fact at exactly the sites the artifact designates as authoritative.
>
> **evidence.** revision_note (:12-14): "TWO of the five records it named as \"named-lineage anchors\" whose monotone inventory result must be re-derived turn out not to be inventory-control models at all -- Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle." — no [corpus-inference].
> Body (:301-307): "it is an inference **from an absence** -- not a statement of Kyle's. **[corpus-inference]** … (i) the extraction read **pp. 1315-1317 only** … so the absence is established over the three pages read and not over the article".
> review §8.4:2225-2228: "**[corpus-inference]** … Every such step is flagged here and in the extraction file, because collapsing the two is the defect this whole thread exists to correct."
> grep "inventory objective" returns 6 sites; only 1 carries the marker.
>
> **fix.** Append "[corpus-inference] for the Kyle half — established over pp. 1315-1317 only, as an inference from absence" to the `revision_note` sentence and to agenda :274 and :313-317. A18 §(g) is append-only; carry the qualifier in the next numbered amendment rather than editing it.
>
> **reference.** Kyle 1985, Econometrica 53(6):1315-1335, doi:10.2307/1913210 (only pp.1315-1317 read); Glosten & Milgrom 1985, JFE 14(1):71-100, doi:10.1016/0304-405X(85)90044-3; CLAUDE.md §Evidence Hierarchy item 5 ("no paraphrasing without verification")

**Refutation evidence.**

> Attempted refutation failed; the finding reproduces exactly at every stated location, and the only mitigation found does not disprove it.
>
> (1) Marker absence reproduced. agenda revision_note :12-14 reads verbatim "TWO of the five records it named as \"named-lineage anchors\" whose monotone inventory result must be re-derived turn out not to be inventory-control models at all -- Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle." with no [corpus-inference] and no page-scope qualifier. agenda :274 "**Neither model has an inventory objective.**" unmarked. agenda :313-317 "vacuous for Glosten & Milgrom and for Kyle, because there is no such result in either model to re-derive" unmarked. protocol A18 §(g) :2738 "**The body establishes two** -- Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle" unmarked.
>
> (2) Rule scope checked, no exemption exists. lit_review :2225-2228 legend quoted accurately. The agenda itself endorses the rule at :279-280 ("the corpus record's section 8.4 legend defines that marker for exactly this step"), and a search of the agenda for any convention exempting front-matter or summary prose returns nothing. So the marker requirement is not scope-limited away from the challenged sites.
>
> (3) No source route to converting the Kyle half into established fact. The body at :301-307 self-characterizes it as "an inference **from an absence** -- not a statement of Kyle's", established over pp. 1315-1317 only, with the extraction row stating "the remaining pages were NOT read". Nothing in the artifact or in Kyle 1985 as read makes the claim author-stated.
>
> (4) Only mitigation found, insufficient. agenda :275-280 immediately qualifies :274 ("The two halves of that claim do **not** rest on the same kind of evidence"), and revision_note :18 carries a pointer "See the supersession markers in Branch 2". Both are cross-references, not markers at the assertion site; :313-317 and the revision_note sentence itself remain flat, and :313-317 is the stated ground for the :341 removal.
>
> (5) Finding is understated, not overstated. The finding's grep count (6 sites, 1 marked) is imprecise: the marked site at :773-778 is invisible to that grep because "no inventory / objective" wraps across lines. Corrected count in the agenda is three marked sites (:302, :341, :773-778) and four unmarked Kyle-inclusive assertions -- including one the finding did not name, :74, "the full texts showed that two records rev 1 had filed under Branch 2 are adverse-selection models with no inventory objective", which asserts full-text establishment more strongly than any site cited. The correction runs against the artifact.

### QUANT-2-4 — retained

- **raised_by:** quant-auditor
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* reporting
- *location:* docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:43 (n_included), :77-123 ("Read this first" limitations block, item 5), :787 (§5 identity table), :891, :2253-2259 (§8.4 strand size)

> **issue.** ADJUDICATION OF THE CENTRAL QUESTION, and the one defect it leaves. Declaring the admitted set as [0,178] while carrying the corpus at 327 IS internally coherent on the substance: §8.4.4 establishes, and I confirmed, that none of the 178 carries a claim line ("none of them supports any sentence in section 8"), and the only §8.4 statement that depends on their admission — "Every universal negative in 8.4 is now weaker" — errs in the conservative direction if the true admitted set is smaller. So Thread A shipped a corpus whose CLAIMS are safe. What it also shipped is an open defect attached to every COUNT, and the conditioning is not carried at the sites where the counts are consumed. `n_included: 327` carries only "WAS 149. 149 + the 178 amendment A16 admitted"; the §5 identity table publishes `n_screened - n_excluded == n_included` → 327 with "holds: yes"; §5 closes "**327** records are included"; §8.4 states "239 records in a 327-record corpus". None of these links to the bound. The disclosure is real but is confined to the frontmatter's `protocol_amendments` prose blob and to §8.4.4. Most consequentially, the block the document itself designates "The four limitations a reader must carry into every number below" carries the 178 at item 5 and describes their warrant as "stronger … than any of the 8,466 classifier dispositions" without the sentence §8.4.4 and A18 §(d) make binding: "A reader must not read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits." That block was corrected under REV-2-2 for precisely this failure mode one session ago.
>
> **evidence.** §8.4.4:3105-3112: "**So the set the transfer clause admits lies in [0, 178] and this artifact narrows it no further.** … **A reader must not read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits.**"
> Frontmatter :43: "n_included: 327   # WAS 149. 149 + the 178 amendment A16 admitted." — no bound reference.
> §5 :787: "| `n_screened - n_excluded == n_included` | 8,813 - 8,486 = **327** | **327** | yes |"
> Mechanical re-verification of every count A18 §(d) asserts, against ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl (192,244 bytes, sha256 8e25fa353dad315c10a7734c0e1c5a627a305b7f1fd05ab1d34406d940ef8848 — matches the published digest): 197 rows; verdicts include 178 / exclude 12 / undecided 7; criterion_cited takes 4 distinct values overall and exactly 2 across the 178 includes, byte-identical boilerplate on 177 and one bespoke value for U01611; 0 of 178 include rows contain any quotation glyph in `rationale`; X5 × 12 and X6 × 0. Every count in A18 §(c)-(d) is exact.
>
> **fix.** Add one clause at each count site: to `n_included`'s comment, to the §5 identity row, to §5's closing sentence and to §8.4's strand-size paragraph — "327 = 149 + 178, where 178 is an UPPER bound (A18 §(c)-(d); §8.4.4); the transfer-clause-admitted set is [0,178] and the corpus size is conditioned on it." And add the sentence verbatim to "Read this first" item 5, which is the block the document tells the reader to carry into every number.
>
> **reference.** protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md A18 §(c)-(d); PRISMA 2020 item 16b (records excluded after assessment); prior finding REV-2-2, which corrected this same block for omitting its two largest limitations

**Refutation evidence.**

> Refutation attempted and failed; every quoted string reproduces byte-for-byte at the stated location in C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md.
>
> (1) Line 43: `n_included: 327   # WAS 149. 149 + the 178 amendment A16 admitted. The 149-record table in section 7 is NOT regenerated: see the note at its head.` — no bound reference. (The finding's excerpt truncates the trailing section-7 clause, which is immaterial: nothing in the omitted text carries the bound.)
> (2) Line 787: `| `n_screened - n_excluded == n_included` | 8,813 - 8,486 = **327** | **327** | yes |` — exact match, no conditioning.
> (3) Line 891: "**327** records are included." — exact; the sentences that follow condition only EXTRACTION DEPTH ("the 178 amendment A16 admitted carry **no extraction at all**"), not admission-set membership.
> (4) Lines 2253-2259: "**239 records in a 327-record corpus**" — exact. This site does carry a cross-reference ("Section 8.4.4 states what the 178 do and do not contribute, which is: they enlarge the corpus and they carry no claim line"), but that pointer asserts the 178 DO enlarge the corpus and characterizes §8.4.4's content as claim-line contribution only; it does not state or point to the [0,178] bound. So it does not falsify "none of these links to the bound" in substance.
> (5) "Read this first" item 5 (lines ~117-123): reproduces exactly, including "a stronger warrant than any of the 8,466 classifier dispositions carry"; it contains no sentence corresponding to §8.4.4's "A reader must not read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits."
> (6) §8.4.4 lines 3105-3112 reproduce the quoted bound verbatim.
>
> Exhaustive counter-search for the conditioning elsewhere: `grep -n "\[0, *178\]\|upper bound\|UPPER bound\|must not read 178\|narrows it no further"` returns exactly four hits — line 29 (frontmatter `protocol_amendments` prose blob), 3105/3108 (§8.4.4), 3850 (§13.1 amendment-digest narrative), 4445 (traceability table row for QUANT-1-2). Confirms the finding's containment claim precisely: the disclosure exists only in the amendments blob, §8.4.4, and two back-matter sites; it reaches none of the four count-consumption sites and not the front block.
>
> I could not construct a counter-test. The one line of attack available — that §8.4's strand paragraph cross-references §8.4.4 — is a partial pointer that does not carry the bound and does not rebut the other three count sites or item 5. The finding's own mechanical re-verification of the A18 §(c)-(d) counts is corroborating, not load-bearing, so no arithmetic error could overturn the claim. Defect stands.

### REPRODUCIBILITY-2-3 — retained

- **raised_by:** reproducibility-verifier
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
