**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* derived counts / internal consistency of the corpus bound
- *location:* docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2253-2259 (§8.4 strand size), :3438-3441 (§8.8), against :3071-3111 (§8.4.4)

> **issue.** The §8.4.4 remediation block declares '178 is an UPPER BOUND, and the artifact records no lower bound above zero' and 'the set the transfer clause admits lies in [0, 178]', then asserts containment: 'Nothing in section 8 rests on any of the 178.' Two section-8 statements falsify that containment on their face — both add the 178 as a point value and report the result as a determinate count. Under the review's own bound their true values are the intervals [61, 239] and [16, 194] over a corpus of [149, 327], and neither site carries the bound.
>
> **evidence.** Mechanical counts against ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl all confirm the adjudication's premises: 198 lines = 1 _header + 197 rows; verdicts include 178 / exclude 12 / undecided 7, matching _header.verdict_counts exactly; criterion_cited over the 178 includes has exactly 2 distinct values, byte-identical boilerplate on 177 and one bespoke value for U01611; no include row carries a verbatim quotation of the record's own text (rationale fields are paraphrase). The bound is therefore real. But lit review:2254-2256 reads '61 of the original 149 records, plus the 178 records amendment A16 admitted ... 239 records in a 327-record corpus', and :3438 reads 'Restated 2026-09-03: the count is now 16 + 178 = 194 of 327.' Both are inside section 8.
>
> **fix.** Either restate both figures as intervals conditioned on §8.4.4 ('61 confirmed plus at most 178, i.e. [61, 239] of a corpus of [149, 327]'), or narrow §8.4.4's containment sentence to say what is true — that no CLAIM LINE rests on the 178 while two CORPUS-SHAPE counts in §8.4 and §8.8 do, and mark those two counts as upper bounds at their sites.
>
> **reference.** none recorded

**Refutation evidence.**

> Could not refute; every premise reproduced and no counter-evidence was obtainable.
>
> VERBATIM REPRODUCTION OF BOTH CITED SITES
> - C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\literature\lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2253-2256 (inside "### 8.4", header at :2209) reads: "S4 is by a wide margin the largest strand: **61 of the original 149 records**, plus the **178** records amendment A16 admitted, all of which are C3 model records and therefore S4 — **239 records in a 327-record corpus**." Point-value addition, determinate count. Confirmed.
> - Same file :3438 (inside "### 8.8", header at :3436) reads: "**Restated 2026-09-03: the count is now 16 + 178 = 194 of 327.**" and :3446 "**59% of the included corpus supports no claim in the synthesis** (194 of 327)". Confirmed. Arithmetic checks: 61+178=239, 16+178=194, 149+178=327.
>
> THE BOUND IS ABSENT AT BOTH SITES
> grep over the whole lit review for "UPPER BOUND|upper bound|[0, 178]|[0,178]" returns hits only at :3071, :3105, :3850, :4444, :4445 — none within §8.4's strand-size paragraph (:2249-2260) or §8.8 (:3436-3450). Both sites carry only a pointer ("Section 8.4.4 states what the 178 do and do not contribute"), never the interval. The finding's "neither site carries the bound" is exactly correct.
>
> MECHANICAL RE-VERIFICATION OF THE AUDITOR'S PREMISES (ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl)
> Ran my own parse: 198 lines = 1 _header + 197 verdict rows; Counter = include 178 / exclude 12 / undecided 7, matching _header.verdict_counts {include:178, exclude_X5:12, undecided_in_subset:7} exactly; distinct criterion_cited among the 178 includes = 2; include rows whose rationale contains any quotation glyph = 0 of 178. All premises reproduce. The [0,178] bound at :3105 is therefore soundly grounded.
>
> THE ARTIFACT'S OWN GOVERNING TEXT CONFIRMS THE DEPENDENCY RATHER THAN SEVERING IT
> I tested the best available refutation — that 327 is a determinate record of the executed screen (identity `n_screened - n_excluded == n_included`, 8,813 - 8,486 = 327, closes at :787) and so is immune to a bound scoped to "the set the transfer clause admits". That defense fails against the protocol's own words. C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\methodology\protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md A18 §(d) states the repair is "re-adjudication to `undecided` of every row for which no such sentence can be quoted — then a restatement of the include count and of every downstream count that uses 327", and closes "Until it is performed, no consumer of this protocol may read 178 as the count of records the transfer clause admits." The protocol thus explicitly classes counts using 327 as downstream of the bounded quantity and pending restatement — which is the finding's claim, not its refutation.
>
> ONE LOCATION INACCURACY, NOT MATERIAL
> The finding attributes the string "Nothing in section 8 rests on any of the 178." to the §8.4.4 block at :3071-3111. That sentence is actually at :1282, in "## 7. Included corpus". However §8.4.4 carries a materially equivalent containment claim in its own text at :3062 — "**none of them supports any sentence in section 8**" — alongside :3057 "They enlarge the corpus and they carry no claim line." So the containment assertion is genuinely present in §8.4.4 and the misattribution of the quoted string's line number does not disturb the defect claim. This is a citation quibble, not counter-evidence.
>
> The strongest thing available in the artifact's favor is a scoping reading: the §7 paragraph at :1283-1285 distinguishes "the corpus's *claims*" from "the corpus's *extent*" and tells the reader extent requires both files; §8.4.4:3057 concedes "they enlarge the corpus"; and the §8.4 standing caveat at :3175 restates that "extent" and "claims" are measured on different populations. That is an interpretive argument that "rests on"/"supports" means claim-support only. It is plausibility, not concrete counter-evidence, and it does not touch the second half of the finding at all — the two corpus-shape counts still stand as point values with no bound at their sites while the review's own protocol says every count using 327 awaits restatement.

### LITERATURE-2-1 — retained

- **raised_by:** literature-check
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* misquotation of a primary source
- *location:* docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2327 and :2967; docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl:26 (record feys2026avellaneda, applicability_conditions_stated[2])

> **issue.** A quoted string attributed to Feys 2026 as the author's own words is not what the paper says. The artifact prints, inside quotation marks and introduced by attributive verbs ("which he calls", "which the author states is", "the author states that"): "the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies". The source sentence substitutes a different noun and drops an attribution clause and an adverb without ellipsis. This is the one Feys quotation that carries the §8.4 central block's revised Feys classification (REV-1-4's own remediation text was written around it), so the misquote sits inside the round-1 fix rather than beside it.
>
> **evidence.** Fetched arXiv:2606.01477 HTML, both v1 and v3 (v3 is the version the extraction log's identity_check names: "arXiv:2606.01477v3"). Remark 1 reads verbatim: "Under the admissibility conditions of Definition 44 (see Appendix B), every strategy π∈Π satisfies W^L_T(π)∈L^∞(ℱ_T); the formal statement and proof are recorded as Lemma 46 in the appendix. The boundedness is the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure machinery of Kupper and Schachermayer (2009) applies directly." The artifact's string replaces "machinery of Kupper and Schachermayer (2009)" with "representation" and drops "directly". Both v1 and v3 fetches returned the identical sentence, so this is not version drift.
>
> **fix.** Replace the quoted string at all three sites with the verbatim sentence, or paraphrase outside quotation marks. If the paraphrase is kept, drop the attributive framing ("which he calls") that presents it as the author's wording.
>
> **reference.** https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01477 (Feys, F.M.V., 2026, v3), Remark 1

**Refutation evidence.**

> Attempted refutation failed. The misquotation reproduces at all three stated sites and the source sentence is verbatim what the auditor reported, confirmed against the exact bytes the corpus itself extracted from.
>
> SOURCE. I fetched three independent renderings of arXiv:2606.01477 myself: https://arxiv.org/html/2606.01477v3 (HTTP 200, 2,018,807 bytes), https://arxiv.org/html/2606.01477v1 (HTTP 200, 1,646,880 bytes), and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.01477 (HTTP 200, 1,115,697 bytes, 63 pages). The PDF's SHA-256 is dc9c2e117469fe0dd0e717ad93c2be494103b99c021bed46f21530bd20992db8 — byte-identical to the sha256 recorded in the extraction log's own retrieval block for record feys2026avellaneda. So the file I searched is not a different version or a different rendering; it is the same object the corpus extracted from.
>
> In all three, Remark 1 (Boundedness of W^L_T) reads: "Under the admissibility conditions of Definition 44 (see Appendix B), every strategy pi in Pi satisfies W^L_T(pi) in L^inf(F_T); the formal statement and proof are recorded as Lemma 46 in the appendix. The boundedness is the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure machinery of Kupper and Schachermayer (2009) applies directly. It can however be relaxed, at the cost of additional integrability conditions on the strategy space."
>
> EXHAUSTIVENESS. The string "regime in which" occurs exactly ONCE in the whole paper in each of the three renderings. "dynamic-risk-measure" occurs exactly twice: the Remark 1 sentence above, and one unrelated sentence ("the mathematical content of AS is therefore not expected-utility maximization but dynamic-risk-measure minimization on liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth"). "dynamic-risk-measure representation" — the artifact's string — occurs ZERO times anywhere in the paper. There is therefore no alternative source sentence the artifact could be quoting, and no version-drift escape: v1 (May 31 2026) and v3 (June 9 2026) carry the identical sentence.
>
> ARTIFACT SITES. All three reproduce verbatim, each under attributive framing that presents the string as the author's own wording:
> - docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2326-2327 — "states that liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth is **bounded** - which he calls *\"the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies\"*. **[FT]**"
> - docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2966-2967 — "liquidation-adjusted terminal wealth is bounded, which the author states is *\"the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies\"*"
> - docs/literature/search_logs/kalshi-arbitrage/ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl:26, record feys2026avellaneda, E10.applicability_conditions_stated, second element - "the author states that 'the boundedness is the regime in which the dynamic-risk-measure representation applies'."
>
> The deviation is exactly as charged: "machinery of Kupper and Schachermayer (2009)" replaced by "representation", and the adverb "directly" dropped, with no ellipsis and no brackets, inside quotation marks introduced by "which he calls" / "which the author states is" / "the author states that".
>
> PLACEMENT CLAIM ALSO HOLDS. Lines 2328-2330 carry the inline note "*(Corrected 2026-09-03, finding REV-1-4: this line previously read \"says nothing about boundedness or endpoint settlement\"...)*", so the misquote does sit inside the round-1 remediation text rather than beside it, as the finding states.
>
> MINOR NOTES THAT DO NOT DEFEAT THE FINDING. (a) The jsonl record has no top-level key named applicability_conditions_stated; it is nested under E10, and the quoted item is the second element (1-based) of that array. The locator is off only in nesting, and the cited content is present exactly where the finding says. (b) The extraction log's identity_check records the preprint as "dated May 30 2026" while arXiv lists v1 as May 31, 2026 — a separate, unrelated discrepancy not part of this finding.
>
> I obtained no counter-evidence of any kind. The defect claim is true.

### LITERATURE-2-3 — retained

- **raised_by:** literature-check
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* method/coding-scheme attribution: E10 code applied against its own frozen definition
- *location:* docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:830 (E10 definition), :2358-2367 (A17 §e), :2501-2519 (A18 §b); docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2866, :2900-2917, :2920, :3152-3155, :3653-3658

> **issue.** The frozen protocol defines E10's transfer status as a property of the record: "does **the record itself** address bounded [0,1] payoffs with terminal settlement at an endpoint: `addressed` / `not addressed` / `explicitly excluded`". The re-execution assigns `explicitly excluded` to Kyle 1985 and Avellaneda & Stoikov 2008 and `addressed` to Glosten & Milgrom 1985, while the same document records at line 2296 that "None of them mentions binary, bounded or event contracts anywhere the corpus read", and marks the compatibility judgements [corpus-inference] at 2870, 2909-2913 and 2934. The label therefore says the author did something the corpus says the author did not do. A18 §(b) — the round-1 REV-1-3 remediation — reasserts the author-grounding in its replacement sentence ("each rested on a sentence the authors wrote"), and §10 G-5 restates "all three `explicitly excluded`" and "which such a payoff **satisfies**" with no marker at all. This is LITERATURE-1-1 one level up, in the governing record rather than the agenda prose, and the round-1 remediation propagated it rather than caught it.
>
> **evidence.** Refutation attempted on the loose reading and failed. If `explicitly excluded` is read loosely as "the record's stated assumptions exclude it", then `addressed` must be read as "the record's stated assumptions admit it" — but the corpus's own Glosten & Milgrom entry says the authors "nowhere discuss binary, bounded or event contracts" and that the satisfaction "is this corpus's inference from the authors' stated condition", so GM does not *address* the question under either reading. Under the strict reading, Kyle and Avellaneda & Stoikov fail. Either way at least one of the three codes is applied against the frozen definition, and the count "three changed and one was confirmed" inherits it. Kyle's cited grounding sentence (p.1317 normality) says nothing about bounded payoffs; A17 §(e)'s Kyle bullet quotes only that sentence.
>
> **fix.** Add an amendment (A19) that records the operative reading of E10 as compatibility-with-the-record's-stated-assumptions rather than record-addresses-the-question, or re-code Kyle and Avellaneda & Stoikov as `not addressed [corpus-inference: incompatible with the stated process assumption]`. Whichever is chosen, add the [corpus-inference] marker at §10 G-5 lines 3653-3658 and qualify A18 §(b)'s "each rested on a sentence the authors wrote" to say which sentence and what the corpus inferred from it.
>
> **reference.** protocol §E10 (line 830); lit_review §8.4 legend (line 2225) defining [corpus-inference] as "a step this corpus takes from something an author states to something the author does not state"

**Refutation evidence.**

> Refutation attempted on four routes; all failed, and each attempt strengthened the finding.
>
> (1) Sought a competing operative definition of the E10 codes elsewhere in the frozen protocol that would license a compatibility reading. `grep -n "explicitly excluded|transfer status|transfer_status|not addressed"` over protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md returns only lines 206, 312, 830, 852 pre-amendment, and none defines the three code values. Line 830 (E10) is the sole definition and is a record-property question: "does the record itself address bounded [0,1] payoffs with terminal settlement at an endpoint". Objective O4 at line 133 restates the same record-property framing verbatim: "whether the source itself addresses bounded [0,1] payoffs with terminal settlement at an endpoint". No amendment A1-A18 redefines the codes; A19 does not exist.
>
> (2) Sought a qualified code value in the frozen extraction file that would show the bare code is not what was recorded. It is what was recorded. ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl gives, verbatim: kyle19851913210 `"transfer_status": "explicitly excluded"` with `corpus_inference_flag` reading "Kyle nowhere discusses binary, bounded or event contracts, and nowhere states that his model does or does not apply to them; the corpus attributes no such statement to him"; avellaneda2008146976807013 `"transfer_status": "explicitly excluded"` with "the authors nowhere discuss bounded-payoff or event contracts"; glosten19850304405x8590 `"transfer_status": "addressed"` with "The authors nowhere discuss binary, bounded or event contracts". The flag field is a disclosure of the inference, not a different code — the record's own flag asserts precisely the state of affairs that the frozen definition codes as `not addressed`.
>
> (3) Reproduced every cited location. lit_review line 2296 ("None of them mentions binary, bounded or event contracts anywhere the corpus read"); [corpus-inference] markers present at 2870, 2909, 2934 and legend at 2225; protocol A17 §(e) 2358-2367 including "Kyle does not say his model excludes binary contracts"; A18 §(b) 2501-2519 replacement sentence "each rested on a sentence the authors wrote".
>
> (4) Counter-checked the "no marker at §10 G-5" sub-claim. `grep -i "inference|infer"` over lit_review lines 3600-3700 returns nothing; the nearest [corpus-inference] marker is at line 3018. G-5 (3653-3658) states "all three `explicitly excluded`" and "which such a payoff **satisfies**" unhedged.
>
> The only reading under which the codes are internally consistent is the compatibility reading, which the frozen text does not state, and which the finding already concedes and names as the fix. Under the frozen text's own reading, the corpus's Kyle entry answers "does the record itself address" with the corpus's own words "nowhere states that his model does or does not apply" — which is the definition of `not addressed`, the code the 2026-09-02 record carried before the change. Nothing obtained refutes the defect claim.

### LITERATURE-2-4 — retained

- **raised_by:** literature-check
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* incomplete marker application (LITERATURE-1-1 remediation)
- *location:* docs/research_notes/research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:74 and :437 (and :274)

> **issue.** The round-1 fix instructed the marker be added "to the Kyle bullet and to the Branch 2 table's Kyle row", and both were done (lines 302, 341, 778). Two further sites state the marked proposition as established fact and were not touched. Line 74: "the full texts showed that two records rev 1 had filed under Branch 2 are adverse-selection models with **no inventory objective**". Line 437, which is Branch 2b's founding premise: "Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle are *adverse-selection* models with zero-expected-profit makers and **no inventory state**." "No inventory state" is verbatim the proposition the Kyle bullet flags as an inference from absence over pp. 1315-1317 only. Line 274 ("**Neither model has an inventory objective.**") is bolded as fact and is qualified only in the bullets below it. A reader who reads Branch 2b without reading Branch 2's superseded-premise block gets exactly the unqualified claim the remediation exists to remove.
>
> **evidence.** grep over docs/ for `inventory objective|no inventory state|reservation price` returns four agenda sites carrying the claim and three carrying the marker; lines 74 and 437 carry neither the marker nor any nearby qualifier. The Branch 2 table's Kyle row (341) and the Verification-status bullet (776-778) are correctly marked, confirming the fix was applied site-by-site rather than by a sweep.
>
> **fix.** At line 437 restate as: "Glosten & Milgrom is an adverse-selection model whose authors state that binding inventory constraints are inconsistent with their zero-profit condition; Kyle carries no inventory state variable in the pages this corpus read — **[corpus-inference]**." At line 74 add the marker or drop "no inventory objective" in favour of "adverse-selection models", which is author-supportable on its own. At line 274 add the marker inline.
>
> **reference.** Round-1 finding LITERATURE-1-1 fix text (docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:602)

**Refutation evidence.**

> No refutation obtained; the finding reproduces at every stated location in C:\Users\skoir\castles\docs\research_notes\research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md.
>
> (1) Line 74 reads verbatim "had filed under Branch 2 are adverse-selection models with no inventory objective, so" — plain assertion, no [corpus-inference] marker and no qualifier anywhere in the enclosing branch-count block (lines 70-79), which I read in full.
>
> (2) Line 437 reads verbatim "with zero-expected-profit makers and no inventory state. Their transfer question is" (sentence begins line 436: "Glosten & Milgrom and Kyle are *adverse-selection* models"). "no inventory state" is the same proposition the Kyle bullet at 300-302 flags as an inference from absence over pp. 1315-1317. Branch 2b (425-470 read in full) carries no marker on it; its own [corpus-inference]-equivalent qualifications concern payoff support ("that inference is the corpus's, not the authors'" for GM; "again the corpus's inference from the author's stated assumption" for Kyle's normality), never inventory.
>
> (3) Counter-check that would have refuted, and does not: Branch 2b's header (lines 431-433) states "**Tier:** T1. **Depth:** **[FT]** for both records." Under the corpus's own legend (docs/literature/lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:2216-2229) [FT] means "rests on the full text" and [corpus-inference] is the distinct marker for "a step this corpus takes from something an author states to something the author does not state." So the branch header positively labels line 437's premise as full-text-grounded, which aggravates rather than cures the unmarked-inference defect.
>
> (4) Marker-site arithmetic verified by grep: `[corpus-inference]` occurs at lines 279 (the correction note), 302 (Kyle bullet), 341 (Branch 2 table Kyle row), 778 (Verification-status bullet). The claim strings occur at 74, 274, 340, 341, 437, 773/776. Lines 74 and 437 carry neither. This matches the finding's characterisation that the round-1 fix was applied site-by-site, and matches the round-1 fix text at docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md:602 ("Add the marker to the Kyle bullet and to the Branch 2 table's Kyle row"), which named only those two sites.
>
> (5) No document-level convention rescues the omission. The only global pointer is inside frontmatter revision_note (line 18, "See the supersession markers in Branch 2"); it is not at either site, and the lit-review rule it inherits (2201-2204, "in 8.4 ... every claim line carries an explicit depth marker") is per-claim-line, not per-proposition-once.
>
> Partial, non-refuting nuance on the parenthetical site only: line 274's bolded "**Neither model has an inventory objective.**" is followed immediately in the same paragraph (lines 274-276) by "The two halves of that claim do **not** rest on the same kind of evidence, and this agenda no longer runs them together," plus the LITERATURE-1-1 correction note — i.e. that site is qualified in its own next sentence, not only "in the bullets below." That weakens the :274 sub-observation but leaves the finding's load-bearing sites (74 and 437) fully intact, and the finding itself already flags :274 as secondary.

### FORMAT-2-1 — retained

- **raised_by:** format-auditor
- **severity_claimed:** major
- **refuted_by:** role `refuter`, effort high
- **evidence_type:** `none`
- **reproduction_required:** false
- **outcome:** retained

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* templates
- *location:* docs/methodology/protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2417-2420 and :2777-2790

> **issue.** A18's opening explicitly promises that the new protocol-with-addendum digest will be published at the end of the entry. It is not. The entry ends with an *Artifacts* block that publishes only the search-log and rule-file digests; the post-append whole-file value `dcfae5643f…` (183,534 bytes) appears nowhere in the protocol. It exists only in two consuming artifacts (lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:3843, :4273 and the agenda frontmatter line 31). A reader holding only the protocol is left with a dangling forward reference, and the superseded value `e93e0151…` is the last digest the protocol itself states — the exact failure mode the frozen-prefix discipline exists to prevent. The promise is also unfulfillable as written: a whole-file digest cannot be written inside the file it digests.
>
> **evidence.** "this append supersedes it and the new value is published at the end of this entry." (:2419-2420); grep for `dcfae5643f` across docs/ returns hits only in lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md and research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md, none in the protocol.
>
> **fix.** Replace the clause with one that is satisfiable and points at the real carrier: "this append supersedes it; the new whole-file value cannot be written into the file it digests and is published by the consuming artifacts (corpus record §13.x and the agenda frontmatter) and by the commit's Repro-Log trailers." Name the consuming sites explicitly so the digest chain is traversable from the protocol.
>
> **reference.** CLAUDE.md §Reproducibility contract (clone-durable digest citation); protocol §10 append-only discipline

**Refutation evidence.**

> Not refuted; the claim reproduces. (1) Quote verified verbatim at protocol_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.md:2419-2420: "this append supersedes it and the new value is published at the end of this entry." (2) sha256sum of the protocol = dcfae5643ff86e49bbcf33a68e730fcb9d7f46b41f30cbd62bafdd297c3fc4dc, 183,534 bytes, exactly as the finding states; grep for "dcfae" inside the protocol returns zero hits, while it appears in lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:3843,:4273 and research_agenda_prediction-market-microstructure_2026-09-02.md:31 — matching the finding's grep claim. (3) The file's final lines are the Artifacts block listing only ka-s4-subset-rule.md, ka-s4-retrieval-recheck-01.json, and four re-verified search logs; no whole-file digest closes the entry. (4) The last protocol-with-addendum digest stated anywhere in the protocol is the superseded e93e0151... (:2418 and :2763). The only counter-evidence candidate I found strengthens rather than refutes the finding: the protocol itself twice states the opposite convention — :1226-1229 and :1795-1799, "It is recorded in the corpus record's section 13.1 and carried by the follow-on provenance commit... it is not written here, because a file cannot carry its own digest." That mitigates only the finding's secondary "dangling forward reference" framing (a general carrier is named elsewhere in the document); the primary defect — A18 promising an in-entry publication that is absent and that the same file declares impossible — is confirmed.

**Claim, verbatim.**

- *category:* identity
- *location:* docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.md (9 occurrences), .json (14), session_trail_2026-09-03.md (2), union_gate_waivers_2026-09-03.md (11)

> **issue.** This round's own tracked audit artifacts embed absolute home-directory paths carrying the OS username (`C:\\Users\\skoir\\castles\\…`, `C:/Users/skoir/…`). Project CLAUDE.md makes repo-relative paths the convention in tracked prose and states the reason in terms; the 2026-08-21 audit already raised and remediated OS-username strings in tracked files as an identity finding (FA-1-01, verified to 0 matches). The practice has regrown to 395 occurrences across 26 tracked files under docs/audits/, and the round-4 deliverables extended it rather than arrested it. The refute-gate memo notices the defect (D9) and explicitly declines to act on it, which converts a known defect into a disclosed-and-perpetuated one.
>
> **evidence.** docs/audits/audit_trail_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.json:959 `"location": "C:\\Users\\skoir\\castles\\docs\\literature\\lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:1587-1599"`; memo §2.3 D9: "absolute home-directory paths appear inside recorded refutation evidence in the tracked trails … noted because CLAUDE.md makes repo-relative paths the convention in tracked prose and this repository is public."; docs/audits/audit_trail_think-tank-charter_2026-08-21.md:186 "OS username removed from tracked files | `grep -c \"skoir\" …` | 0".
>
> **fix.** Normalise paths to repo-relative in the round-4 trail (md + json) and in the session_trail / union_gate_waivers writers before commit; add the rewrite at the emitting hook so it does not regrow. Backfilling the 22 historical files is optional and should be recorded as a separate decision, but the artifacts this round emits must not add to the count.
>
> **reference.** castles CLAUDE.md §Identity hygiene (final paragraph); docs/audits/audit_trail_think-tank-charter_2026-08-21.md FA-1-01

**Refutation evidence.**

> Refutation failed; every component reproduces. (1) Cited location verbatim: docs/audits/audit_trail_kalshi-arbitrage-review_2026-09-02.json:959 is `"location": "C:\\Users\\skoir\\castles\\docs\\literature\\lit_review_kalshi-arbitrage_2026-09-02.md:1587-1599",`. (2) docs/audits/audit_trail_think-tank-charter_2026-08-21.md:186 carries the FA-1-01 row `| OS username removed from tracked files | grep -c "skoir" docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_*.md | 0 |` as quoted. (3) docs/research_notes/memo_refute-gate-calibration_2026-09-03.md:181-184 carries D9 with the quoted text; `grep -n "D9"` returns exactly one hit in the file, and the only action recorded is "This memo quotes only passages that do not contain them" — no remediation. "Notices and declines to act" is accurate. (4) Counts via regex C:[\\/]{1,2}Users[\\/]{1,2}skoir — s4 trail .md=9 (claim 9), s4 trail .json=16 (claim 14, understated), session_trail_2026-09-03.md=2 (claim 2), union_gate_waivers_2026-09-03.md=11 (claim 11). Repo-wide under docs/audits/: 453 occurrences across 26 files (claim 395/26; occurrence count understated, file count exact). Both variants present: escaped backslash inside JSON strings and forward-slash in union-gate writer lines (e.g. union_gate_waivers_2026-09-03.md:7 `... | C:/Users/skoir/castles/docs/deliverables/gate_waiver_0dd6aec6-5e0.md`). Counter-angles tested and rejected: (a) No waiver or ADR authorizes absolute paths — `grep -rniE "absolute path" docs/decisions/ docs/audits/union_gate_waivers_*.md docs/methodology/` returns zero hits. (b) CLAUDE.md:115 does state the convention ("Repo-relative paths remain the convention in tracked prose"), though its stated rationale is portability, "regardless of identity policy", and §Identity hygiene forbids the address rather than the name/username — this makes the `identity` category label debatable but is a classification quibble, not refutation of the convention violation. (c) session_trail_2026-09-03.md and union_gate_waivers_2026-09-03.md are currently untracked (??), but docs/audits/ is not gitignored and all sibling files are tracked, so they are pending-commit deliverables, not excluded. (d) FA-1-01's verification command was scoped to docs/deliverables/deliverable_spec_*.md rather than repo-wide, but the finding quotes that command verbatim alongside the label, so it does not misrepresent the source, and a narrower prior remediation does not negate the present occurrences. No concrete counter-evidence obtained; finding stands.

| # | claim under verification | disposition this round |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The central adjudication — 197 rows, 178/12/7, two distinct `criterion_cited` values with boilerplate on 177, X6 rate 0 by construction, 0 of 178 quoting the record's own text, so the screen is declared too permissive and 178 an UPPER bound with range [0,178] | Counts reproduce exactly against `ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl` on three independent branches. The adjudication is correct and against the session's own interest. Coherence question answered: shipping the corpus at 327 while bounding the admitted set at [0,178] IS coherent, because no §8 claim line rests on the 178 and the only affected statement errs conservatively — so Thread A shipped a corpus, not an open defect with a corpus attached. The residual defect is PLACEMENT: the bound reaches four back-matter sites and none of the count-consumption sites (REV-2-1, QUANT-2-4, REPRODUCIBILITY-2-3, SCOPE-2-1, SCOPE-2-2). |

## residual-risk-and-sampling-caveat

**Sampling caveat.** This is an audit, and an audit SAMPLES. The evidence examined in this round is not guaranteed to be representative of the artifacts as a whole: no branch read every line of every artifact, several checks were purposive rather than random, and the absence of a finding in a region no branch examined is not evidence that the region is clean. Specific, measured instances of that limit in this round: 58 of the 72 explosive-regime records have never been read at any verification round, and the measured error rate on re-read texts gives no ground for assuming the remainder is clean; only 21 of 140 retained refute-gate dispositions were read in full for the calibration memo; the Thread C load-bearing set covers 45 of a section-7 candidate pool the branch showed to be miscounted; and two of this round's three highest-effort branches could not execute code or render image-only PDFs, so parts of their scope were checked by inspection rather than by reproduction.

**Residual risks not covered by any finding.** Recorded per branch, verbatim in the sidecar under `residual_risks`; summarised here.

- **critical-reviewer.** RQI coverage. (1) Importance of the question — ADEQUATE, no finding: the four threads answer questions the artifacts themselves raised (a screen left incomplete at 4.7x its included set, two repository reproducibility gaps, locator verification on load-bearing claims, and whether a 7.3% gate kill rate means anything), and each is worth asking. (2) Originality — ADEQUATE, explicit note: this is a corrective/verification deliverable rather than a novel result, and it says so at every level; no known result is restated as new. The one genuinely novel object is the calibration memo's H3 (the gate's rate is conditional on triage and is not commensurable with a raw-candidate rate), which is demonstrable from the trail alone and is correctly flagged as such. (3) Method, (4) Presentation, (5) Interpretation — covered by the findings above. Verification of the ten claims the brief asked me to check. Claim 1 (the central adjudication) — every mechanical count reproduces against ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl; the adjudication is correct and against interest; the incoherence is placement, not logic (REV-2-1). Claim 2 (QUANT-1-3's proposed repair rejected) — the rejection is correct on its own evidence: recoding U01126 SR-2d would code as unretrievable a record the recheck retrieved, and the other three each hit HTTP 429 on two of six arms, which this corpus's binding rule forbids reading a…
- **scope-auditor.** Every declared deliverable in the spec exists on disk and is reachable: both Thread A logs (`ka-s4-completion-screen.jsonl`, `ka-s4-fulltext-extraction.jsonl`), the revised review and agenda, `uv.lock` (confirmed tracked — the reflog shows commit 2ba291f9922547e1848d7d505cedafb2979e2433 "build: track uv.lock as the pinning mechanism; ADR-0005", which independently upholds the round-1 refutation of SCOPE-1-1 and is why it is not re-raised), ADR-0005, `tests/test_erob_recount.py` (commit 01ecfe78), `se-verify-loadbearing-01.json`, the calibration memo, and the Thread E audit trail. No `omitted` finding. The supporting artifacts round 1's remediation left behind — `ka-s4-subset-rule.md`, `ka-s4-subset-membership.json`, the two retrieval logs — are instrumental to declared deliverables and named in the Delegation block, so they are not scope creep. The residual risk is not absence but WARRANT: the spec's largest item was checked by counts that the delivery now says do not measure what the item promised, and the only thing keeping that from contaminating the synthesis is a second declaration ("Nothing in section 8 rests on any of the 178") that lives in prose and is enforced by no test. If a successor session extends the CSL-JSON store to 327, or writes a claim line against any of the 178, the [0,178] bound becomes load-bearing silently and nothing in the repository will fire. Secon…

I traced every allow-list member to an exercising site (module scope 1/9/10 to `parents[1]`, `range(1,9)`, `range(1,10)`; each `read_*` entry to its `Match.group` call; `test_partial_extension...{0,1,7}` to `answered = 0`, `+= 1`, `QUESTIONS[:7]`/`[7:]`), so the third assertion should pass without a dead entry, and no allow-list is a superset of what the file uses. ## immutability-and-retention

This trail is committed to the repository and is **append-only**. Round 1's front matter, findings table, refute-gate entries and JSON sidecar are unmodified by this round: round 2 is appended below them and carries its own front matter and its own sidecar (`docs/audits/audit_trail_s4-reexecution-and-repo-gaps_2026-09-03.round2.json`), so no previously recorded information is obscured (21 CFR 11.10(e)).

**Corrections.** No entry in this trail is ever edited or deleted. A correction is made by appending a dated addendum under a heading of the form `## addendum YYYY-MM-DD — <finding id or section>`, which quotes the superseded text verbatim, states what is wrong with it and states the corrected value. The superseded text stays legible. This is the same strike-by-quotation mechanism the governing protocols use for their append-only amendments, and it is what makes a later reader able to see both what was recorded and what was later found.

**Retention.** The trail and its sidecars are retained for the life of the repository as the durable audit record of this deliverable. The sidecar holds this round's payload verbatim and machine-readable (FAIR I1); the markdown is the human-readable rendering of the same payload, compressed only in the findings table, where every truncation is marked `…` and resolvable against the sidecar. Both are tracked in git; the git history is the tamper-evidence.
